\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{\text{\rm BO}}^{2}&\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2-2\rho}\frac{|n|^{2s+2\rho}}{|n_{1}n_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{1}{|n|^{2\rho-2}|n-n_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}}<\infty,\end{split}

(5.5)

provided that

\displaystyle\rho\geq 1,\quad s\geq-\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{3}{2}-2\rho.

(5.6)

\bullet

Case (i.b):

n,n_{1}>0>n_{2}
.

In this case, we have
|n_{1}|>|n_{2}|
and thus
|n_{1}|\gtrsim|n|
.
When
s\geq 0
, it follows from Lemma
2.1
that

\displaystyle A_{\text{\rm BO}}^{2}\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|n|^{2\rho-2}|n_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}<\infty,

(5.7)

provided that (
5.6
) holds.

Next,
we consider the case
s<0
.
We separately consider the cases
|n_{1}|\sim|n_{2}|
and
|n_{1}|\sim|n|
(
\gg|n_{2}|
).
When
|n_{1}|\sim|n_{2}|
,
it follows from Lemma
2.1
that

\displaystyle A_{\text{\rm BO}}^{2}\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|n|^{2\rho-2s-2}|n_{2}|^{4s+2\rho}}<\infty,

(5.8)

provided that

\displaystyle\rho\geq s+1,\quad s\geq-\frac{1}{2}\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{3}{2}-2\rho.

(5.9)

When
|n_{1}|\sim|n|
,
(
5.5
) holds under
(
5.6
).

A simple computation shows that (
5.6
)
and (
5.9
) (with
s<0
for the latter)
are satisfied under (
1.22
).
Hence,
from Lemma
3.1
(i),
we conclude that

X^{\text{\rm BO}}\in\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})
under (
1.22
).

(ii)
Write
X^{\text{\rm BO}}=X^{\text{\rm BO},1}+X^{\text{\rm BO},2}
,
where
X^{\text{\rm BO},1}
is the contribution from
|n_{1}|\geq|n_{2}|
in (
5.3
)
and
X^{\text{\rm BO},2}=X^{\text{\rm BO}}-X^{\text{\rm BO},1}
.
We only consider
X^{\text{\rm BO},1}
since a similar bound holds for
X^{\text{\rm BO},2}
by symmetry.
Since
|n_{1}|\geq|n_{2}|
,
we only need to consider the cases (a), (b), and (e) in Part (i).
Moreover, by symmetry,
it suffices to consider the cases (a) and (b).

As before, we have

\displaystyle\|X^{\text{\rm BO},1}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2,1}^{s,s_{0}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{(1)}_{\text{\rm BO}}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.10)

where
A^{(1)}_{\text{\rm BO}}
is given by

\displaystyle A^{(1)}_{\text{\rm BO}}=\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\\
|n_{1}|\geq|n_{2}|\end{subarray}}\langle n\rangle^{2{s_{0}}+2}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{2{s_{0}}}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{2s}|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|^{2\rho}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.11)

\bullet

Case (ii.a):

n_{1}n_{2}>0
.

In this case, we can proceed as in Case (i.a)
and obtain

A^{(1)}_{\text{\rm BO}}<\infty
under (
1.22
).

\bullet

Case (ii.b):

n,n_{1}>0>n_{2}
.

When
s_{0}\geq 0
or
|n_{1}|\sim|n|
,
we can proceed as in Case (i.b)
and obtain

A^{(1)}_{\text{\rm BO}}<\infty
under (
5.6
).
Thus, it suffices to consider the case

s_{0}<0
and
|n_{1}|\sim|n_{2}|
.
From (
5.11
) and Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle(A_{\text{\rm BO}}^{(1)})^{2}\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|n|^{2\rho-2s_{0}-2}|n_{2}|^{2s_{0}+2s+2\rho}}<\infty,

provided that

\displaystyle\rho\geq s_{0}+1,\quad s_{0}+s\geq-\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{3}{2}-2\rho

which is satisfied under (
1.22
).
Hence,
from Lemma
3.1
(ii),
we conclude that, if (
1.22
) holds,
then

X^{\text{\rm BO}}\in\mathcal{Y}^{s,s_{0},\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})

for any
s_{0}>s
.

(iii) As in Part (i),
by symmetry,
we only consider Cases (a) and (b).
As before, we have

\displaystyle\|X^{\text{\rm BO}}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s};H^{s_{0}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{(2)}_{\text{\rm BO}}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma},

where
A^{(2)}_{\text{\rm BO}}
is given by

\displaystyle A^{(2)}_{\text{\rm BO}}=\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\langle n\rangle^{2{s_{0}}+2}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{2{s}}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{2s}|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|^{2\rho}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.12)

\bullet

Case (iii.a):

n_{1}n_{2}>0
.

Without loss of generality, assume that
|n_{1}|\geq|n_{2}|
.
Then, we have
|n_{1}|\gtrsim|n|
under
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
.
In view of Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle(A_{\text{\rm BO}}^{(2)})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2s_{0}-2s+2-2\rho}\frac{|n|^{2s+2\rho}}{|n_{1}n_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\frac{1}{|n|^{2\rho-2s_{0}+2s-2}|n-n_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}}<\infty,

provided that

\displaystyle s_{0}\leq s+\rho-1,\quad s\geq-\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s_{0}<2s+2\rho-\frac{3}{2}.

(5.13)

\bullet

Case (iii.b):

n,n_{1}>0>n_{2}
.

When
s_{0}\geq 0
or
|n_{1}|\sim|n|
,
we can proceed as in Case (iii.a)
and obtain

A^{(2)}_{\text{\rm BO}}<\infty
under (
5.6
).
Thus, it suffices to consider the case

s_{0}<0
and
|n_{1}|\sim|n_{2}|
.
From (
5.12
) and Lemma
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle(A_{\text{\rm BO}}^{(2)})^{2}\lesssim\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|n|^{2\rho-2s_{0}-2}|n_{2}|^{4s+2\rho}}<\infty,

provided that

\displaystyle s_{0}\leq\rho-1,\quad s\geq-\frac{1}{2}\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s_{0}<2s+2\rho-\frac{3}{2}.

(5.14)

By separately considering the cases
s\geq 0
and
s<0
,
we obtain (
1.27
)
from (
5.13
) and (
5.14
).
Hence,
from Lemma
3.1
(iii),
we conclude that

X\in\mathcal{X}^{s,s_{0},\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})
under (
1.27
).

(iv)
By proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
(iv)
with (
4.13
),
we see that the claim follows
from a slight modification of Part (i),
provided that

\displaystyle\rho>1,\quad s>-\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{3}{2}-2\rho.

and

\displaystyle\rho\geq s+1,\quad s>-\frac{1}{2}\rho,\quad\text{and}\quad s>\frac{3}{2}-2\rho.

where the latter set of the conditions is for
s<0
.
Note that these conditions are satisfied
if
\rho>1
and
s>-\frac{1}{2}\rho
.

This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

5.2.
Modulated ILW equation

In this subsection, we consider
the well-posedness and related issues for
the modulated ILW (
1.17
)
and the modulated scaled ILW (
1.20
)
for fixed
0<\delta<\infty
.
Since these two equations are related by
the scaling transform (
1.19
),
we only consider the modulated ILW (
1.17
).

Fix
0<\delta<\infty
. The bilinear driver associated with
the modulated ILW (
1.17
) is given by

\displaystyle X^{\delta}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})=\int_{r}^{t}U^{w}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t^{\prime})f_{1})(U^{w}(t^{\prime})f_{2})\big)dt^{\prime},

(5.15)

where

\displaystyle U^{w}(t)=e^{w(t)\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}

(5.16)

denotes
the modulated linear propagator
for (
1.17
).
By taking the Fourier transform, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\big(X^{\delta}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\big)(n)=in\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n}))\widehat{f}_{1}(n_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(n_{2})

(5.17)

where
\Phi_{t,r}^{w}
is as in (
1.6
)
and

\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})
denotes the resonance function for ILW given by

\displaystyle\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})=\Xi_{\delta}(n,n_{1},n_{2})=-p_{\delta}(n)+p_{\delta}(n_{1})+p_{\delta}(n_{2}).

(5.18)

Here,
p_{\delta}(n)
denotes the multiplier for
-i\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}
given by

\displaystyle p_{\delta}(n)=n^{2}\coth(\delta n)-\frac{n}{\delta}.

(5.19)

See (
1.18
).
Then, under
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})=-n^{2}\coth(\delta n)+n_{1}^{2}\coth(\delta n_{1})+n_{2}^{2}\coth(\delta n_{2}).

(5.20)

By applying

[
23
, (2.7)]

and
the fact that
x^{\alpha+1}\leq C_{\alpha}(e^{2x}-1)
for any
x\geq 0

and
\alpha\geq 0
,
we have

\displaystyle n^{2}\coth(\delta n)-|n|n=\sgn(n)\cdot\frac{2n^{2}}{e^{2|\delta n|}-1}=O(\delta^{-2})

(5.21)

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
Hence, from (
5.4
), (
5.20
) and (
5.21
),
we have

\displaystyle\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})=\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})+O(\delta^{-2}),

(5.22)

uniformly in
\bar{n}=(n,n_{1},n_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{*})^{3}
.
Then, a slight modification of the proof of Proposition
5.1

for the modulated BO
yields the following proposition
on the driver
X^{\delta}
for the modulated ILW,
from which
Theorem
1.11
(ii) and Theorem
1.17
(iii) follow.

Proposition 5.2
.

Given
\rho\geq 1
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
Fix
0<\delta<\infty
.

(i)

Suppose that
\rho\geq 1
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

satisfy
(
1.22
).
Then,
the driver
X^{\delta}
defined in (
5.15
)
belongs to
\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})

defined in (
2.9
).

(ii)

(persistence of regularity).

Suppose that
\rho\geq 1
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

satisfy
(
1.22
).
Then,
for any
s_{0}>s
,
the driver
X^{\delta}

belongs to

\mathcal{Y}^{s,s_{0},\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})

defined in (
2.14
).

(iii)

(nonlinear smoothing).

In addition, suppose that
s_{0}>s
satisfies
(
1.27
).
Then,
the driver
X^{\delta}

belongs to

\mathcal{X}^{s,s_{0},\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})

defined in (
2.13
).

(iv)

(Galerkin approximation).

Define the truncated driver
X^{\delta,N}

as in (
4.7
),
where we replace

U^{w}(t)
by the modulated linear propagator
for ILW defined in (
5.16
).
Suppose that
\rho>1
and
s>-\frac{1}{2}\rho
.
Then,
the truncated driver
X^{\delta,N}

converges
to
X^{\delta}

in
\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})
as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

We only show how the claim in (i)
follows as a modification of the proof of
Proposition
5.1
(i).
The claims in (ii), (iii), and (iv) follow from
similar modifications.

Proceeding as in (
4.8
) (see also (
4.9
)),
we have

\displaystyle\|X^{\delta}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{\delta}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

(5.23)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq T
,
where
A_{\delta}
is given by

\displaystyle A_{\delta}=\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\langle n\rangle^{2s+2}\frac{1}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{2s}\langle\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})\rangle^{2\rho}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.24)

From (a)-(e) in the proof of Proposition
5.1
(i)
we have

\displaystyle|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|\gtrsim\max(|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)

(5.25)

for any
\bar{n}=(n,n_{1},n_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{*})^{3}

with
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
.
In particular, from (
5.22
) and (
5.25
),
we obtain

\displaystyle|\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})|\geq|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|-O(\delta^{-2})\sim|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|

(5.26)

when

\max(|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gg\delta^{-2}
.
Now, write

\displaystyle A_{\delta}=A_{\delta}^{1}+A_{\delta}^{2},

(5.27)

where

A_{\delta}^{1}
is the restriction
of
A_{\delta}
to the case

\max(|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|)\gg\delta^{-2}

and
A_{\delta}^{2}=A_{\delta}-A_{\delta}^{1}
.
Then, from (
5.24
), (
5.26
),
and
the proof of Proposition
5.1
(i),
we have

\displaystyle A_{\delta}^{1}<\infty,

(5.28)

provided that (
1.22
) holds.
On the other hand, by a crude estimate,
we have

\displaystyle A_{\delta}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sup_{|n_{1}|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\sum_{|n|\lesssim\delta^{-2}}\frac{\langle n\rangle^{2s+2}}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle n-n_{1}\rangle^{2s}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim 1+\delta^{-6|s|-3}<\infty.

(5.29)

Hence,
from Lemma
3.1
(i)
with (
5.23
),
(
5.27
), (
5.28
), and (
5.29
),
we conclude that

X\in\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})
under (
1.22
).
∎

Remark 5.3
.

It is clear from the proof
that
the claims in
Proposition
5.2

(such as the size of the
\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}(T)
-norm, convergence speed, etc.)
are uniform in
\delta\gg 1
.
However, there is no uniform control
for
0<\delta\ll 1
, which
presents an additional difficulty in establishing
shallow-water convergence
(as compared to deep-water convergence);
see Subsection
5.4
.

5.3.
Deep-water convergence of the modulated ILW

In this subsection, we establish
deep-water convergence (
\delta\to\infty
) of the modulated ILW (
1.17
)
to the modulated BO (
1.16
)
(Theorem
1.13
(i)).

Given
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
,
let
u^{\delta}
,
0<\delta<\infty
, and
u
denote the local-in-time solutions
to the modulated ILW (
1.17
)
and the modulated BO (
1.16
)
with
u^{\delta}|_{t=0}=u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
, respectively.
Let

\mathbf{u}^{\delta}(t)=e^{-w(t)\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}u^{\delta}(t)

and

\mathbf{u}(t)=e^{-w(t)\mathcal{H}\partial_{x}^{2}}u(t)

be the modulated interaction representations
of
u^{\delta}
and
u
(under the modulated ILW and the modulated BO, respectively).
Then, by the unitarity of
e^{-w(t)\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}
on
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
,
we have

\displaystyle\|u^{\delta}-u\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}\leq\|\mathbf{u}^{\delta}-\mathbf{u}\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}+\|(e^{-w(t)\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}-e^{-w(t)\mathcal{H}\partial_{x}^{2}})u\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

(5.30)

From the mean value theorem and
[
93
, Lemma 2.1]
, we have,
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\big((e^{-w(t)\mathcal{G}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}-e^{-w(t)\mathcal{H}\partial_{x}^{2}})f\big)(n)

\displaystyle=|w(t)|\big|n^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}(n)-(-i|n|n)\big||\widehat{f}(n)|

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0,

as
\delta\to\infty
.
Then, from the dominated convergence theorem,
we see that the second term on the right-hand side
of (
5.30
) tends to
0

as
\delta\to\infty
.

Hence,
Theorem
1.13
(i)
follows once we show

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to\infty}\|\mathbf{u}^{\delta}-\mathbf{u}\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}=0.

(5.31)

In view of
Lemma
3.1
(iv), Remark
3.2
(i), and
Proposition
3.8
(iii),
it then suffices to prove

\displaystyle\|X^{\delta}_{t,r}-X^{\text{\rm BO}}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.32)

as
\delta\to\infty
,
uniformly in
0\leq r<t\leq T
,
where
X^{\delta}
and
X^{\text{\rm BO}}

are as in
(
5.15
) and
(
5.1
), respectively.

Proceeding as in (
4.8
) with (
5.17
) and (
5.3
)
we have

\displaystyle\|X_{t,r}^{\delta}-X^{\text{\rm BO}}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}\langle n\rangle^{2s+2}\frac{|\Phi^{w}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi^{w}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n}))|^{2}}{\langle n_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle n_{2}\rangle^{2s}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.33)

From
(
1.5
) and (
5.22
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n}))|&\leq|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n}))|+|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n}))|\\
&\lesssim\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}|t-r|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})\rangle^{\rho}}\end{split}

(5.34)

for any
\delta\gg 1
and

0\leq r<t\leq T
.
On the other hand,
from (
1.6
), (
5.22
),
and the mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n}))|&=\bigg|\int_{r}^{t}e^{i\Xi_{\rm BO}(\bar{n})w(t^{\prime})}\big(e^{iO(\delta^{-2})w(t^{\prime})}-1\big)dt^{\prime}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\delta^{-2}|t-r|.\end{aligned}

(5.35)

By interpolating (
5.34
) and (
5.35
),
we have

\displaystyle|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n}))|\lesssim_{T}\delta^{-2\varepsilon}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}^{1-\varepsilon}|t-r|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})\rangle^{\rho(1-\varepsilon)}}

(5.36)

for any
0\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
.
Hence,
by a slight modification of (
5.5
), (
5.7
), and (
5.8
)
(where we replace
\rho
by
\rho(1-\varepsilon)
for small
\varepsilon>0
)
with (
5.33
) and (
5.36
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\|X_{t,r}^{\delta}-X^{\text{\rm BO}}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\delta^{-2\varepsilon}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}^{1-\varepsilon}|t-r|^{\gamma}=o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

as
\delta\to\infty
,
uniformly in
0\leq r<t\leq T
,
provided that

\displaystyle\rho>1\quad\text{and}\quad s>-\frac{1}{2}\rho.

This proves (
5.32
) (and (
5.31
))
and thus concludes the proof of Theorem
1.13
(i).

5.4.
Shallow-water convergence of the modulated scaled ILW

In this subsection,
we establish shallow-water
convergence (
\delta\to 0
) of the modulated scaled ILW
(
1.20
) to the modulated KdV (
1.2
)
(Theorem
1.13
(ii)).
In the previous subsection,
we proved the deep-water convergence
by viewing the modulated ILW
as a perturbation of the modulated BO.
In the shallow-water regime,
we can not employ this simple perturbative viewpoint
and thus, we need to carry out careful analysis
on the resonance function
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})

for the scaled ILW (see (
5.41
)).
In fact, in the low frequency regime,
we view the modulated scaled ILW
as a perturbation of the modulated KdV,
while
we view it
as a perturbation of the modulated BO
in the high frequency regime.
When there is a mixture of high and low frequencies,
we proceed with a direct computation,
exhibiting an extra smoothing property.
In the remaining part of this section,
we assume that
0<\delta\ll 1
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}
be as in (
1.21
) (see also (
1.18
)).
Then,
the Fourier multiplier
\widetilde{p}_{\delta}(n)
of
-i\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{\delta}(n)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{\delta}p_{\delta}(n)=n^{3}\big(1-h(n,\delta)\big),

(5.37)

where
p_{\delta}(n)
is as in (
5.19
) and
h(n,\delta)
is given by
16
16

16

Recall that

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k^{2}}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}
.

\displaystyle h(n,\delta)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{6\delta^{2}n^{2}}{\pi^{2}k^{2}(\pi^{2}k^{2}+\delta^{2}n^{2})}\in(0,1).

(5.38)

See
[
1
, Lemma 8.2.1]
,

[
93
, (the proof of) Lemma 2.3]
,
and
[
24
, Lemma 2.3]
.
Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then,
from (
5.38
), we have

\displaystyle h(n,\delta)\lesssim\delta^{2}N^{2}

(5.39)

for any
|n|\leq N
.

Let
\widetilde{X}^{\delta}
be the bilinear driver associated with
the modulated scaled ILW (
1.20
), given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})=\int_{r}^{t}U^{w}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t^{\prime})f_{1})(U^{w}(t^{\prime})f_{2})\big)dt^{\prime},

(5.40)

where
U^{w}(t)=e^{w(t)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}
.
By taking the Fourier transform, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\big(\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\big)(n)=in\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n}))\widehat{f}_{1}(n_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(n_{2})

where
\Phi_{t,r}^{w}
is as in (
1.6
)
and

\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})
denotes the resonance function for the scaled ILW given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})&=\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(n,n_{1},n_{2})=-\widetilde{p}_{\delta}(n)+\widetilde{p}_{\delta}(n_{1})+\widetilde{p}_{\delta}(n_{2})\\
&=-n^{3}\big(1-h(n,\delta)\big)+n_{1}^{3}\big(1-h(n_{1},\delta)\big)+n_{2}^{3}\big(1-h(n_{2},\delta)\big).\end{split}

(5.41)

Given
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
,
let
v^{\delta}
,
0<\delta\ll 1
, and
u
denote the local-in-time solutions
to the modulated scaled ILW (
1.20
)
and the modulated KdV (
1.2
)
with
v^{\delta}|_{t=0}=u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
, respectively.
Let

\mathbf{v}^{\delta}(t)=e^{-w(t)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}v^{\delta}(t)

and

\mathbf{u}(t)=e^{w(t)\partial_{x}^{3}}u(t)

be the modulated interaction representations
of
v^{\delta}
and
u
(under the modulated scaled ILW and the modulated KdV, respectively).
Then, by the unitarity of
e^{-w(t)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}
on
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
, we have

\displaystyle\|v^{\delta}-u\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}\leq\|\mathbf{v}^{\delta}-\mathbf{u}\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}+\|(e^{-w(t)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}-e^{w(t)\partial_{x}^{3}})u\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

(5.42)

From the mean value theorem and
[
93
, Lemma 2.3]
, we have,
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\big((e^{-w(t)\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\delta}\partial_{x}^{2}}-e^{w(t)\partial_{x}^{3}})f\big)(n)

\displaystyle=|w(t)|\big|n^{2}\widehat{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}_{\delta}(n)-(-in^{3})\big||\widehat{f}(n)|

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0,

as
\delta\to 0
.
Then, from the dominated convergence theorem,
we see that the second term on the right-hand side
of (
5.42
) tends to
0

as
\delta\to\infty
.
Hence,
Theorem
1.13
(ii)
follows once we show

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\|\mathbf{v}^{\delta}-\mathbf{u}\|_{C_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}=0.

(5.43)

As in the case of the deep-water convergence,
in view of Lemma
3.1
(iv), Remark
3.2
, and
Proposition
3.8
(iii),
(
5.43
) follows once we prove

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{t,r}-X^{\text{\rm KdV}}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.44)

as
\delta\to 0
,
uniformly in
0\leq r\leq t\leq T
,
where
X^{\text{\rm KdV}}

is the driver for the modulated KdV defined in (
4.3
).

From (
4.5
) and (
5.41
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n}):=\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})-\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})=n^{3}h(n,\delta)-n_{1}^{3}h(n_{1},\delta)-n_{2}^{3}h(n_{2},\delta).

(5.45)

Then, from (
5.39
),
and (
5.45
), we have

\displaystyle|\Delta\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})|\lesssim\delta^{2}N^{5},

(5.46)

uniformly in
|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})\rangle\sim\langle\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})\rangle,

(5.47)

uniformly in
|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N

and
\delta\ll N^{-\frac{5}{2}}
.
Then,
from (
1.5
) and (
5.47
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|&\leq|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n}))|+|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|\\
&\lesssim\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}|t-r|^{\gamma}\frac{1}{\langle\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})\rangle^{\rho}}\end{split}

(5.48)

uniformly in
|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N
,

\delta\ll N^{-\frac{5}{2}}
,
and

0\leq r<t\leq T
.
Moreover,
from (
1.6
), (
5.46
), and the mean value theorem,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|&=\bigg|\int_{r}^{t}e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w(t^{\prime})}\big(e^{i\Delta\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\overline{n})w(t^{\prime})}-1\big)dt^{\prime}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\delta^{2}N^{5}|t-r|,\end{aligned}

(5.49)

uniformly in
|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N
.
By interpolating (
5.48
) and (
5.49
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n}))-\Phi_{t,r}^{w}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N\}}\\
&\quad\lesssim_{T}\delta^{2\varepsilon}N^{5\varepsilon}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}^{1-\varepsilon}|t-r|^{\gamma}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{\{|n|,|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N\}}}{\langle\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})\rangle^{\rho(1-\varepsilon)}}.\end{aligned}

(5.50)

for any
0\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
.

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
X^{\text{\rm KdV},N}_{t,r}

be the frequency-truncated driver
defined in (
4.7
).
Similarly,
let
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N}_{t,r}

be
the frequency-truncated version of
\widetilde{X}^{\delta}
in (
5.40
).
Then, proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
(i)
(where we replace
\rho
by
\rho(1-\varepsilon)

for small
\varepsilon>0
)
with (
5.50
),

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N}_{t,r}-X^{\text{\rm KdV},N}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}({H^{s}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\delta^{2\varepsilon}N^{5\varepsilon}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}^{1-\varepsilon}|t-r|^{\gamma}=o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.51)

as
\delta\to 0
,
uniformly in
0\leq r<t\leq T
,
provided that
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

satisfy
(
1.25
).
From the proof of
Proposition
4.1
(iv), we have

\displaystyle\|X^{\text{\rm KdV},N}_{t,r}-X^{\text{\rm KdV}}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}

\displaystyle\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.52)

as
N\to\infty
,
uniformly in
0\leq r<t\leq T
.
Hence, by the triangle inequality with (
5.51
)
and (
5.52
),
the bound (
5.44
)
follows once we prove the following uniform bound:

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\ll 1}\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta}_{t,r}-\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.53)

as
N\to\infty
.

Let us first take a close look at the resonance function

\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})
defined in (
5.41
).
A direct computation with (
5.37
) and (
5.38
)
yields

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})=-6nn_{1}n_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\pi^{2}k^{2}\big(3\pi^{2}k^{2}+\delta^{2}(n_{1}^{2}+n_{1}n_{2}+n_{2}^{2})\big)}{\prod_{j=0}^{2}(\pi^{2}k^{2}+\delta^{2}n_{j}^{2})}

(5.54)

under
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
, where
n_{0}:=n
.
Write
|n|
,
|n_{1}|
, and
|n_{2}|

in the non-increasing order and denote them by

n_{\max}
,

n_{\med}
, and
n_{\min}
, respectively.

\bullet

Case 1:

n_{\max}\lesssim\delta^{-1}
.

Denote by

\widetilde{X}^{\delta,(1)}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N,(1)}

the contributions to

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N}
, respectively,
from the frequency region
n_{\max}\lesssim\delta^{-1}
.
In this case, from (
5.54
), we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})|\sim|nn_{1}n_{2}|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\pi^{2}k^{2}}\sim|nn_{1}n_{2}|\sim|\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})|,

(5.55)

uniformly in
0<\delta\ll 1
.
Hence, by repeating the proof of Proposition

4.1
(iv) with (
5.55
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\ll 1}\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta,(1)}_{t,r}-\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N,(1)}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.56)

as
N\to\infty
,
provided that

\rho>\frac{1}{2}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

satisfy
(
1.25
).

\bullet

Case 2:

n_{\max}\sim n_{\min}\gg\delta^{-1}
.

Denote by

\widetilde{X}^{\delta,(2)}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N,(2)}

the contributions to

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N}
, respectively,
from the frequency region

n_{\max}\sim n_{\min}\gg\delta^{-1}
.
From (a)-(e) in the proof of Proposition
5.1
(i)
we have

\displaystyle|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|\sim n_{\max}n_{\min}\sim n_{\max}^{2}.

(5.57)

Then,
from (
5.41
), (
5.37
),
(
5.18
), (
5.22
), and (
5.57
),
we obtain

\displaystyle|\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})-\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|\ll n_{\max}^{2}\sim|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|,

from which we conclude that

\displaystyle|\Xi_{\delta}(\bar{n})|\sim|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|\quad\text{and thus}\quad|\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})|\sim\delta^{-1}|\Xi_{\text{\rm BO}}(\bar{n})|,

(5.58)

uniformly in
0<\delta\ll 1
.
Hence, by repeating the proof of Proposition

5.1
(iv) with (
5.58
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\ll 1}\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta,(2)}_{t,r}-\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N,(2)}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.59)

as
N\to\infty
,
provided that

\rho>1
and
s>-\frac{1}{2}\rho
.

\bullet

Case 3:

n_{\max}\gg\max(n_{\min},\delta^{-1})
.

Denote by

\widetilde{X}^{\delta,(3)}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N,(3)}

the contributions to

\widetilde{X}^{\delta}
and
\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N}
, respectively, in this case.
Assume that
|n_{1}|\leq|n_{2}|
.
Since
n_{\med}\sim n_{\max}
under
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
,
it follows from
(
5.54
) with
n_{1}n_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}n_{1}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}n_{2}^{2}
that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})|\sim|nn_{1}n_{2}|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\pi^{2}k^{2}}{\prod_{j=0}^{1}(\pi^{2}k^{2}+\delta^{2}n_{j}^{2})}

Without loss of generality, assume that
|n|=|n_{0}|\leq|n_{1}|
.
Then,
by crudely estimating from below by an integral, we have

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\Xi}_{\delta}(\bar{n})|

\displaystyle\sim|nn_{1}^{3}n_{2}|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\pi^{2}(\frac{k}{n_{1}})^{2}}{\prod_{j=0}^{1}\big(\pi^{2}(\frac{k}{n_{1}})^{2}+\delta^{2}(\frac{n_{j}}{n_{1}})^{2}\big)}

\displaystyle\geq|nn_{1}^{4}n_{2}|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\pi^{2}(\frac{k}{n_{1}})^{2}}{\big(\pi^{2}(\frac{k}{n_{1}})^{2}+\delta^{2}\big)^{2}}\frac{1}{|n_{1}|}

\displaystyle\geq|nn_{1}^{4}n_{2}|\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{\pi^{2}x^{2}}{(\pi^{2}x^{2}+\delta^{2})^{2}}dx

\displaystyle\geq|nn_{1}^{4}n_{2}|\int_{1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\pi^{2}x^{2}}dx\sim|nn_{1}^{4}n_{2}|

\displaystyle\gg|\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})|,

uniformly in
0<\delta\ll 1
.
Hence, by repeating the proof of Proposition

4.1
(iv) with (
5.55
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\delta\ll 1}\|\widetilde{X}^{\delta,(3)}_{t,r}-\widetilde{X}^{\delta,N,(3)}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s})}\lesssim o(1)|t-r|^{\gamma},

(5.60)

as
N\to\infty
,
provided that

\rho>\frac{1}{2}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

satisfy
(
1.25
).

Putting
(
5.56
),
(
5.59
), and
(
5.60
) together,
we obtain (
5.53
),
provided that

\rho>1
and
s>-\frac{1}{2}\rho
.
Therefore, under the same condition,
we obtain
(
5.44
)
from the triangle inequality, (
5.51
), (
5.52
), and (
5.53
),
where we first take
\delta\to 0
and then
N\to\infty
.
This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.13
(ii).

6.
Global well-posedness in

L^{2}

In this section,
our main goal is to establish the following proposition on the conservation of the
L^{2}
-norm
of a solution to the modulated dispersive equations
we considered in Sections
4
and
5
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Given
\rho>0
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
Given
s\geq 0
, suppose that
the driver
X
defined in
(
4.3
) belongs to

X\in\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})

and that

u
is a solution to the modulated KdV equation (
1.2
) on
\mathbb{T}
, whose
modulated interaction representation
\mathbf{u}
defined in (
1.7
)
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}([0,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
,
satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}=u_{0}+\mathcal{I}^{X}(\mathbf{u}).

(6.1)

Here,
\mathcal{I}^{X}(\mathbf{u})
denotes the nonlinear Young integral of
\mathbf{u}

with the driver
X
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{L^{2}}=\|u(0)\|_{L^{2}}

(6.2)

for any
0\leq t\leq T
.

With appropriate minor modifications
on the driver space

X\in\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}([0,T]\times\mathcal{M})
,
the
L^{2}
-conservation (
6.2
) also holds
for the modulated KdV equation on
\mathbb{R}
,
the modulated mKdV, BO, ILW, and scaled ILW equations on
\mathbb{T}
.

Once we prove
Proposition
6.1
,
global well-posedness in
H^{s}(\mathcal{M})
,
s\geq 0
,
holds
for
the following cases:

(i)

the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{T}

for
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,

(ii)

the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}

for
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,

(iii)

the modulated BO (
1.16
),
the modulated ILW (
1.17
),
and the modulated scaled ILW (
1.20
) on
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{T}

for
\rho\geq 1

(Theorem
1.11
(ii)).

These global well-posedness results
follow from Proposition
6.1

together with
the persistence-of-regularity results (Proposition
3.8
(ii)
with
Propositions
4.1
(ii),

4.4
(ii),

5.1
(ii),
and
5.2
(ii))
which state that the local existence
time of an
H^{s}
-solutions depend
only on the
L^{2}
-norm of initial data
(beside the
\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)
-norm of the associated driver
X
).
Since the argument is standard, we omit details.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

We only consider the periodic modulated KdV case.
The claim for the other equations follows
from a similar computation.

Let
X
be as in
(
4.3
).
For simplicity, we set
X_{t,r}(f)=X_{t,r}(f,f)
.
Then,
from the unitarity of
U^{w}(t)
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\langle f,X_{t,r}(f)\rangle_{L^{2}}&=\int_{r}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f\cdot U^{w}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t^{\prime})f)^{2}\big)dxdt^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{r}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}}U^{w}(t^{\prime})f\cdot\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t^{\prime})f)^{2}\big)dxdt^{\prime}\\
&=\frac{1}{3}\int_{r}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t^{\prime})f)^{3}\big)dxdt^{\prime}=0\end{split}

(6.3)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq T
and
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T})
.
Consequently,
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\mathbf{u}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}&=\|\mathbf{u}(r)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2\big\langle\mathbf{u}(r),X_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}(r))\big\rangle_{L^{2}}+\|\mathbf{u}(t)-\mathbf{u}(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+R_{t,r}\\
&=\|\mathbf{u}(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\mathbf{u}(t)-\mathbf{u}(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+R_{t,r},\end{aligned}

(6.4)

where

\displaystyle R_{t,r}=2\big\langle\mathbf{u}(r),\mathbf{u}(t)-\mathbf{u}(r)-X_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}(r))\big\rangle_{L^{2}}.

By assumption, we have
we have

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))

with
\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
.
Then,
from (
6.1
) and (
3.43
) in Proposition
3.7

(see also (
3.17
)),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|R_{t,r}|&\leq\|\mathbf{u}(r)\|_{L^{2}}\|\mathbf{u}(t)-\mathbf{u}(r)-X_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}(r))\|_{L^{2}}\\
&=\|\mathbf{u}(r)\|_{L^{2}}\|(\updelta\mathcal{I}^{X}(\mathbf{u}))_{t,r}-X_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}(r))\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\lesssim C\big(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}\big)\|X\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)}|t-r|^{2\gamma}.\end{split}

(6.5)

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}(t)-\mathbf{u}(r)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{u}\|_{C^{\gamma}_{T}L^{2}_{x}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

(6.6)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq T
.
Hence, from (
6.4
), (
6.5
), and (
6.6
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\big|\|\mathbf{u}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\|\mathbf{u}(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\big|\lesssim C\big(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}_{T}L^{2}_{x}},\|X\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)}\big)|t-r|^{2\gamma}.

(6.7)

By recalling
2\gamma>1
,
the
L^{2}
-conservation (
6.2
)
then follows from (
6.7
).
∎

7.
Global well-posedness of the modulated KdV in negative Sobolev spaces

In this section, we prove global well-posedness of
the periodic modulated KdV (
1.2
) in negative Sobolev spaces
(Theorem
1.3
(ii)).
Our strategy is to adapt
the so-called
I
-method (
=
the method of almost conservation laws),
introduced by Colliander, Keel, Staffilani, Takaoka, and Tao

[
31
]
, to the current modulated setting.

Fix
s<0

in the remaining part of this section.
Given
N\geq 1
,
we define a smooth, even,
non-increasing (in
|\xi|
)
function

m_{s,N}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};[0,1])

by setting

\displaystyle m_{s,N}(\xi)=\begin{cases}1,&\text{for }|\xi|\leq N,\\
\frac{|\xi|^{s}}{N^{s}}.&\text{for }|\xi|\geq 2N.\end{cases}

(7.1)

Then, we define the so-called
I
-operator
I=I_{s,N}

to be the Fourier multiplier operator with the multiplier
m_{s,N}
:

\displaystyle\widehat{I_{s,N}f}(\xi)=m_{s,N}(\xi)\widehat{f}(\xi).

(7.2)

Note that

I_{s,N}
acts as the
i
dentity operator on low frequencies
\{|\xi|\leq N\}
,
while it acts as a (fractional)

i
ntegration operator
(recall
s<0
)
on high frequencies
\{|\xi|\geq 2N\}

with the following bound:

\displaystyle\|f\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}\leq\|If\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim N^{-s}\|f\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})},

(7.3)

and hence the name
\boldsymbol{I}
-method.

We proved local well-posedness
of
the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on
\mathbb{T}

by applying the abstract subcritical local well-posedness
result (Proposition
3.8
).
Thus, it follows from from the blowup alternative (
3.50
)
in Remark
3.9
(i)
that
global well-posedness of the periodic modulated KdV follows
once we show that the
H^{s}
-norm
of a solution
u(t)
remain finite
for each finite time
t>0
.
In view of (
7.3
),
it then suffices to control the so-called
modified energy
\|Iu(t)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
.
17
17

17

As we see below, given a target time
T>0
,
we in fact apply a scaling first and study the scaled modified energy in (
7.28
).

In Subsection
7.1
,
we go over basic scaling properties
of the periodic modulated KdV.
In Subsection
7.2
,
we then study local well-posedness
of the so-called (scaled) modulated
I
-KdV equation (
7.20
).
After establishing a crucial commutator estimate
(Proposition
7.6
) in Subsection
7.4
,
we establish global well-posedness
by combining the commutator estimate (Proposition
7.6
)
with the sewing lemma (Lemma
3.3
).
It is worthwhile to note that we need to make use
of the sewing lemma even in the globalization argument via the
I
-method
which is a new feature in the current modulated setting.
We also discuss the real line case in Subsection
7.5
.

7.1.
Scaling properties of the modulated KdV

In this subsection, we discuss
the scaling properties
of the modulated KdV (
1.2
)
on
\mathbb{T}

which will be used to make the problem at hand
into a small data problem; see (
7.10
), (
7.49
), and (
7.51
).
We first recall the scaling invariance
of the (unmodulated) KdV (
1.3
);
a function
u
is a solution to KdV (
1.3
) on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}

if and only if
the scaled fucntion

\displaystyle u^{\lambda}(t,x)=\lambda^{-2}u(\lambda^{-3}t,\lambda^{-1}x)

(7.4)

is a solution to KdV on
[0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
,
where

\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}=\mathbb{R}/(2\pi\lambda)

denotes the dilated circle.
We note that, unlike the real line setting,
the spatial domain is dilated under the scaling (
7.4
).

In the current modulated setting,
we formally see that

u
is a solution to the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}
with initial data
u_{0}

if and only if

u^{\lambda}
in (
7.4
)
is a solution to the
following scaled modulated KdV on
[0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u^{\lambda}=\partial_{x}^{3}u^{\lambda}\cdot\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}+\partial_{x}(u^{\lambda})^{2}

(7.5)

with the scaled initial data

\displaystyle u_{0}^{\lambda}(x)=\lambda^{-2}u_{0}(\lambda^{-1}x),

(7.6)

where the scaled modulation
w^{\lambda}
is given by

\displaystyle w^{\lambda}(t)=\lambda^{3}w(\lambda^{-3}t)

(7.7)

such that
\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}(t)=\partial_{t}w(\lambda^{-3}t)
.
Namely, the scaling (
7.7
) on the modulation function
is
\dot{W}^{1,\infty}
-invariant.
See Lemma
7.3

for equivalence of the unscaled and scaled modulated KdV equations.
In the following, we study the scaled modulated KdV (
7.5
) on
the dilated circle
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
with
\lambda\geq 1
.

Before proceeding further, we
introduce some notations.
Set

\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}=\mathbb{Z}/\lambda

and

\mathbb{Z}^{*}_{\lambda}=\mathbb{Z}^{*}/\lambda
.
We define
the Fourier transform of a function
f
on
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}

by

\widehat{f}(n)=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}}f(x)e^{-inx}\frac{dx}{2\pi}

for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}f(x)&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}}\widehat{f}(n)e^{inx},\\
\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}}|f(x)|^{2}\frac{dx}{2\pi}&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}}|\widehat{f}(n)|^{2},\\
\widehat{fg}(n)&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}\widehat{f}(n_{1})\widehat{g}(n_{2}).\end{split}

(7.8)

Given
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
we define the non-homogeneous and homogeneous Sobolev spaces
H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})

and
\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})

via the (semi-)norms:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|f\|^{2}_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}}\langle n\rangle^{2s}|\widehat{f}(n)|^{2},\\
\|f\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}&=\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}}|n|^{2s}|\widehat{f}(n)|^{2}.\end{split}

(7.9)

When
\lambda=1
,
the
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
-norm and
\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T})
-norm
are equivalent in the current mean-zero setting.
When
\lambda\gg 1
,
we still have

\|f\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\sim_{\lambda}\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}

for a mean-zero function
f
on
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}

but the implicit constant diverges as
\lambda\to\infty
.
In the following,
we will work with the homogeneous Sobolev spaces,
since they enjoy a better scaling property;
noting from (
7.6
) that

\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}}(u_{0}^{\lambda})(n)=\lambda^{-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}}(u_{0})(\lambda n)
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}
,
we have

\displaystyle\|u_{0}^{\lambda}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}=\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-s}\|u_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T})}

(7.10)

for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
while, for the non-homogeneous Sobolev space, we have

\displaystyle\|u_{0}^{\lambda}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\leq\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-s}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}

for
s\leq 0
; see also Remark
7.2
.
In particular, when
s>-\frac{3}{2}
(namely, in the scaling subcritical regime),
we can make
the
\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})
-norm of the scaled initial data
u_{0}^{\lambda}

small by choosing
\lambda\gg_{\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}}1
.

Now, we are ready to study the scaled modulated KdV (
7.5
) on
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}

with the scaled initial data
u_{0}^{\lambda}
in (
7.6
).
The Duhamel formulation
for the modulated interaction representation

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)=U^{w^{\lambda}}(t)^{-1}u^{\lambda}(t)

(7.11)

is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)=u^{\lambda}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}U^{w^{\lambda}}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w^{\lambda}}(t^{\prime})\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t^{\prime}))^{2}\big)dt^{\prime}

(7.12)

with the associated bilinear driver
X^{\lambda}

given by

\displaystyle X_{t,r}^{\lambda}(f_{1},f_{2})=\int_{r}^{t}U^{w^{\lambda}}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w^{\lambda}}(t^{\prime})f_{1})(U^{w^{\lambda}}(t^{\prime})f_{2})\big)dt^{\prime}

(7.13)

for functions
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
on
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
.
Then, local well-posedness of the scaled modulated KdV (
7.5
)
in
\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})

follows from Proposition
3.8

and the following lemma.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
,
and let
X^{\lambda}
be as in (
7.13
).
Suppose that
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

satisfy
(
1.9
).
Then,
the driver
X^{\lambda}
in (
7.13
)
belongs to
\dot{\mathcal{X}}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})
defined in (
2.11
),
satisfying the bound
:

\displaystyle\|X^{\lambda}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}+s-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

(7.14)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
.

Proof.

Proceeding as in
(
4.8
) with (
7.8
) and (
7.9
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}=\lambda^{-3}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}|n|^{2s+2}\bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))\widehat{f}_{1}(n_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(n_{2})\bigg|^{2}\\
&\quad\leq\lambda^{-1}\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2s+2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|^{2}}{|n_{1}|^{2s}|n_{2}|^{2s}}\bigg)\|f_{1}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}\|f_{2}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2},\end{aligned}

(7.15)

where
\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}
and

\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})

are
as in (
1.6
) and (
4.5
),
respectively.
From (
1.6
),
(
7.7
),
(
1.5
), and (
4.6
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))&=\bigg|\int_{r}^{t}e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w^{\lambda}(t^{\prime})}dt^{\prime}\bigg|=\lambda^{3}\bigg|\int_{\lambda^{-3}r}^{\lambda^{-3}t}e^{i\lambda^{3}\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w(t^{\prime})}dt^{\prime}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-3\rho-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}|nn_{1}n_{2}|^{-\rho}\end{aligned}

(7.16)

for any
n,n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}
, satisfying
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
.

Given
n,n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}
,
let
m=\lambda n
and
m_{j}=\lambda n_{j}
,
j=1,2
.
Then, by
proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
4.1
(i)
with
(
7.15
)
and (
7.16
),
we have

\displaystyle\|X_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{5}{2}-3\rho-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2-4\rho}\frac{|n|^{2s+2\rho}}{|n_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}|n_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}+s-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(\sup_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m,m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
m_{1}=m-m_{2}\end{subarray}}|m|^{2-4\rho}\frac{|m|^{2s+2\rho}}{|m_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}|m_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}+s-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
provided that
(
1.9
) holds.
Hence,
from Lemma
3.1
(i)
and Remark
3.2
(ii),
we conclude that

X^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})
under (
1.9
).
∎

Remark 7.2
.

By repeating the proof for the non-homogeneous Sobolev space
H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})

with

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\langle m\rangle\lesssim\langle n\rangle\lesssim\langle m\rangle,

we obtain

\displaystyle\|X^{\lambda}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-s-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma},

for
\lambda\geq 1
,
which gives a worse power of
\lambda
as compared to (
7.14
),
yielding a worse regularity threshold
for global well-posedness on the unscaled modulated KdV (
1.2
).

The next lemma shows equivalence of the
unscaled and scaled problems.
See Definition
1.2

for the definition of the class

\dot{\mathscr{D}}_{w}^{s}(I\times\mathcal{M})
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
Fix
s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying (
1.9
)
and
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
.
Then,
u\in\dot{\mathscr{D}}_{w}^{s}([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{T})
is a solution to the modulated KdV (
1.2
)
on
[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{T}

with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}

for some
0<\tau\leq T

if and only if the scaled function
u^{\lambda}
defined in (
7.4
)
belongs to
\dot{\mathscr{D}}_{w}^{s}([0,\lambda^{3}\tau]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})

and is a solution to the scaled modulated KdV (
7.5
)
on
[0,\lambda^{3}\tau]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}

with the scaled initial data

u^{\lambda}|_{t=0}=u_{0}^{\lambda}
defined in (
7.6
).

Proof.

We only show the forward direction.
Suppose that

u\in\dot{\mathscr{D}}_{w}^{s}([0,\tau]\times\mathbb{T})

is a solution to the modulated KdV (
1.2
)
on
[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{T}

with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}
.
From Definition
1.2
,
the modulated interaction representation

\mathbf{u}(t)=U^{w}(t)^{-1}u(t)

belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}([0,\tau];\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
.
Then,
a direct computation with
(
7.4
) shows that

\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)=U^{w^{\lambda}}(t)^{-1}u^{\lambda}(t)\in\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}([0,\lambda^{3}\tau];\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})).

Namely,

u^{\lambda}\in\dot{\mathscr{D}}_{w}^{s}([0,\lambda^{3}\tau]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})
.

Now, we need to check that
u^{\lambda}
is indeed a solution
to the scaled modulated KdV (
7.5
)
on the time interval
[0,\lambda^{3}\tau]

in the sense that its modulated interaction representation

\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}
satisfies (
7.12
),
where we interpret the integral as a nonlinear Young integral.

Let
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
be functions on
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
.
Then, from
(
7.13
)
with
(
7.8
),
(
1.6
),
(
7.7
),
a change of variables,
(
4.5
),
and
(
4.4
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}}\big(X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\big)(n)=\frac{in}{\lambda}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}_{\lambda}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))\widehat{f}_{1}(n_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(n_{2})\\
&\quad=in\lambda^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
\lambda n=m_{1}+m_{2}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{w}_{\lambda^{-3}t,\lambda^{-3}r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\lambda n,m_{1},m_{2}))\widehat{f}_{1}(\lambda^{-1}m_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(\lambda^{-1}m_{2})\\
&\quad=\lambda^{-1}\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{T}}\big(X_{\lambda^{-3}t,\lambda^{-3}r}(f_{1}^{\lambda^{-1}},f_{2}^{\lambda^{-1}})\big)(\lambda n)\end{aligned}

(7.17)

for any

n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}

and
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}\tau
,
where
m_{j}=\lambda n_{j}
and
f_{j}^{\lambda^{-1}}(x)=\lambda^{2}f_{j}(\lambda x)
is a function on
\mathbb{T}
,
j=1,2
.
Thus, from (
7.17
) with (
7.4
), we have

\displaystyle X_{t,r}^{\lambda}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))(x)=\lambda^{-2}X_{\lambda^{-3}t,\lambda^{-3}r}(\mathbf{u}(\lambda^{-3}r),\mathbf{u}(\lambda^{-3}r))(\lambda^{-1}x)

(7.18)

for
x\in\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
.
Since
\mathbf{u}
is a solution to (
7.12
)
on the time interval
[0,\tau]

with
\lambda=1
,
where we interpret
the integral as a nonlinear Young integral,
it follows from (
3.43
) in Proposition
3.7

(adapted to the homogeneous Sobolev space) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}(\lambda^{-3}t)-\mathbf{u}(\lambda^{-3}r)-X_{\lambda^{-3}t,\lambda^{-3}r}(\mathbf{u}(\lambda^{-3}r),\mathbf{u}(\lambda^{-3}r))\|_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T})}\lesssim|t-r|^{2\gamma}

(7.19)

for any
0\leq\lambda^{-3}r<\lambda^{-3}t\leq\tau
.
Then, from (
7.19
) with
(
7.4
), (
7.18
), and
(
7.10
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)-\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r)-X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))\|_{\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\lesssim\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-s}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}\tau
.
Let
\Theta_{t,r}=X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))
.
Noting that
2\gamma>1
,
we then obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)-u_{0}^{\lambda}=\lim_{|\Pi([0,t])|\to 0}\sum^{n}_{j=0}\Theta_{t_{j},t_{j+1}}=\mathcal{I}^{X^{\lambda}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda})(t)

for any
0<t\leq\lambda^{3}\tau
, where
the limit is in the sense of Lemma
3.3
(iii)
and the second equality follows from the uniqueness
of the nonlinear Young integral with the driver
X^{\lambda}
.
This proves that

\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}
is a solution to (
7.12
)
on
[0,\lambda^{3}\tau]
.
∎

7.2.
Modulated
I
-KdV equation

In this subsection, we go over local well-posedness
of the following scaled modulated
I
-KdV
on the dilated circle
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}Iu^{\lambda}=\partial_{x}^{3}Iu^{\lambda}\cdot\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}+\partial_{x}I\big((u^{\lambda})^{2}\big).

(7.20)

By writing

\partial_{x}I\big((u^{\lambda})^{2}\big)=\partial_{x}I\big((I^{-1}Iu^{\lambda})(I^{-1}Iu^{\lambda})\big)
,
we define the associated bilinear driver
Y^{\lambda}
by setting

\displaystyle Y^{\lambda}(f_{1},f_{2})=IX^{\lambda}(I^{-1}f_{1},I^{-1}f_{2})

(7.21)

for functions
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
on
\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}
,
where
X^{\lambda}
is as in (
7.13
).

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
,
and and let
Y^{\lambda}
be as in (
7.21
).
Then, for any
s<0

satisfying (
1.9
),
the driver
Y^{\lambda}
in (
7.21
)
belongs to
\mathcal{X}^{0,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})
defined in (
2.9
),
satisfying the bound
:

\displaystyle\|Y^{\lambda}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

(7.22)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
.

Proof.

In view of Lemma
3.1
(i),
it suffices to prove (
7.22
).
Moreover, by the interpolation lemma (see
[
32
, Lemma 12.1]
),
it suffices to prove (
7.22
) for
N=1
.

Proceeding as in (
7.15
) with (
7.21
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|&Y^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&=\lambda^{-3}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}|n|^{2}\bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}M(\bar{n})\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))\widehat{f}_{1}(n_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(n_{2})\bigg|^{2}\\
&\leq\lambda^{-1}\bigg(\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n,n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
n_{1}=n-n_{2}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2}|M(\bar{n})|^{2}|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|^{2}\bigg)\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}\|f_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2},\end{aligned}

(7.23)

where
M(\bar{n})=M(n,n_{1},n_{2})
is defined by

\displaystyle M(\bar{n})=M(n,n_{1},n_{2})=\frac{m_{s,1}(n)}{m_{s,1}(n_{1})m_{s,1}(n_{2})}.

(7.24)

Then,
by comparing (
7.23
) with (
7.15
),
the bound
(
7.22
) follows from a slight modification of the proof of Lemma
7.1

with

\displaystyle M(\bar{n})\lesssim\lambda^{-s}\frac{|n|^{s}}{|n_{1}|^{s}|n_{2}|^{s}}

for any
n,n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}

with
n=n_{1}+n_{2}
,
which follows from (
7.24
), (
7.1
), and case by case analysis
(recall that
s<0
).
∎

By noting that we have

\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}\leq 1

for any
\lambda\geq 1

in (
7.22
),
it follows from Lemma
7.4
and
Lemma
3.1
(i)
(more precisely, see (
2.9
) and (
2.10
))
that

\displaystyle\|Y^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{0,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\lesssim\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}},

(7.25)

where the implicit constant is independent of
\lambda\geq 1
.
Then,
as a corollary to Lemma
7.4
(with (
7.25
))
and Proposition
3.8
,
we obtain the following local well-posedness
result for the scaled modulated
I
-KdV (
7.20
).

Corollary 7.5
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular
on
[0,T]

in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
Fix
s<0
satisfying (
1.9
).
Then, given any

v_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})
and
t_{0}\in[0,\lambda^{3}T]
,
there exist
C_{0}>0
,
\theta>0
, both independent of
v_{0}
and
t_{0}
,
and a unique solution
Iu

to
the scaled modulated
I
-KdV equation (
7.20
)
with
Iu^{\lambda}(t_{0})=v_{0}

on the time interval
[t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]\cap[0,\lambda^{3}T]
,
belonging to the class
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{w}^{0}\big(([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]\cap[0,\lambda^{3}T])\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}\big),

where the local existence time

\tau=\tau\big(\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\big)>0
satisfies

\displaystyle\tau\geq C_{0}\|Y^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{0,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{-\theta}\big(1+\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\big)^{-\theta}.

(7.26)

Moreover, given any
0<\alpha<\gamma
,
there exists
C_{\alpha}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|Iu^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau];L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}\leq C_{\alpha}\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}.

(7.27)

7.3.
Commutator estimate

In view of the blowup alternative (
3.50
)
with (
7.3
) and (
7.10
),
our main task is to control the growth
of the (scaled) modified energy:

\displaystyle\|Iu^{\lambda}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}=\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2},

(7.28)

where
\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}
denotes the modulated interaction representation of
u^{\lambda}

defined in (
7.11
).
Recall from (
6.3
) that

\displaystyle\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}=0.

(7.29)

Using (
7.29
),
we then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}-\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&\quad=\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\com_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}+R^{\lambda}_{t,r}\end{split}

(7.30)

for any
t>r\geq 0
,
where the commutator
\com_{t,r}
and the remainder
R^{\lambda}_{t,r}
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\com_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})&=IX^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})-X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(If_{1},If_{2}),\\
R^{\lambda}_{t,r}&=\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)-I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}\\
&\quad+2\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)-I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r)-IX^{\lambda}_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}.\end{split}

(7.31)

In the next proposition,
we establish a key commutator estimate,
controlling the first term on the right-hand side of (
7.30
).
In particular, note that the bound (
7.32
)
on the commutator is controlled by a negative power of
N
(see (
7.33
)),
yielding almost conservation of the modified energy

\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}
,
which is the reason that this approach is called the method
of almost conservation laws.

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular
on
[0,T]

in the sense of Definition
1.1
,
and let
X^{\lambda}
be as in (
7.13
).
Fix
s<0
satisfying (
1.9
).
Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\com_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}&=\|IX^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})-X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(If_{1},If_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&\lesssim K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}\|If_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\|If_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\end{aligned}

(7.32)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
\lambda\geq 1
, and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where
K(\lambda,N)
is given by

\displaystyle K(\lambda,N)=\begin{cases}\lambda^{3-3\rho-3\gamma}N^{\frac{3}{2}-3\rho},&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<\rho<\frac{3}{2}$},\\
\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}N^{-3}\sqrt{\log N+\log\lambda},&\text{if $\rho=\frac{3}{2}$},\\
\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-2\rho-3\gamma}N^{-2\rho},&\text{if $\rho>\frac{3}{2}$}.\end{cases}

(7.33)

Proof.

From (
7.13
) with (
7.2
) and (
7.8
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|&IX^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})-X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(If_{1},If_{2})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&=\lambda^{-3}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}|n|^{2}\bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}M_{N}(\bar{n})\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))\widehat{f}_{1}(n_{1})\widehat{f}_{2}(n_{2})\bigg|^{2},\end{aligned}

(7.34)

where
\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}
and

\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})

are as in (
1.6
)
and (
4.5
), respectively,
and

\displaystyle M_{N}(\bar{n})=M_{N}(n,n_{1},n_{2})=m_{s,N}(n)-m_{s,N}(n_{1})m_{s,N}(n_{2}).

(7.35)

Then, given
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}
,
we split the hyperplane
\Sigma_{n}=\big\{(n_{1},n_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*})^{2}:n=n_{1}+n_{2}\big\}
into four regions:

\displaystyle\Sigma_{n}=\bigcup_{j=1,2,3,4}D_{j}(n),

where
D_{j}(n)
,
j=1,\dots,4
, is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} D_{1}(n)&=\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2})\in\Sigma_{n}:\,|n_{1}|<\frac{N}{2},|n_{2}|<\frac{N}{2}\Big\},\\
D_{2}(n)&=\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2})\in\Sigma_{n}:\,|n_{1}|\geq\frac{N}{2},|n_{2}|<\frac{N}{2},|n|<\frac{N}{4}\Big\},\\
D_{3}(n)&=\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2})\in\Sigma_{n}:\,|n_{1}|\geq\frac{N}{2},|n_{2}|<\frac{N}{2},|n|\geq\frac{N}{4}\Big\},\\
D_{4}(n)&=\Big\{(n_{1},n_{2})\in\Sigma_{n}:\,|n_{1}|\geq\frac{N}{2},|n_{2}|\geq\frac{N}{2}\Big\}.\end{aligned}

(7.36)

On
D_{1}(n)
, we have
M_{N}(\bar{n})=0
,
since

m_{s,N}(n)=m_{s,N}(n_{1})=m_{s,N}(n_{2})=1
.

Proceeding as in (
4.8
) and (
4.30
)
with (
7.9
),
we have

LHS of (
7.34
)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=2}^{4}A_{\lambda,N}^{(j)}\|If_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}\|If_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}

where
A_{\lambda,N}^{(j)}
,
j=2,3,4
, is given by

\displaystyle A_{\lambda,N}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
0<|n|<\frac{N}{4}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2}\sup_{D_{2}(n)}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|^{2}|M_{N}(\bar{n})|^{2}}{|m_{s,N}(n_{1})|^{2}|m_{s,N}(n_{2})|^{2}},

(7.37)

\displaystyle A_{\lambda,N}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n|\geq\frac{N}{4}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2}\sum_{D_{3}(n)}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|^{2}|M_{N}(\bar{n})|^{2}}{|m_{s,N}(n_{1})|^{2}|m_{s,N}(n_{2})|^{2}},

(7.38)

\displaystyle A_{\lambda,N}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(n)}\cdot|n|^{2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n}))|^{2}|M_{N}(\bar{n})|^{2}}{|m_{s,N}(n_{1})|^{2}|m_{s,N}(n_{2})|^{2}}.

(7.39)

\bullet

Case 1:
on
D_{2}
.

In this case, we have
|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\sim N
.
In particular, we have
|m_{N}(n)|\sim|m_{N}(n_{j})|\sim 1
,
j=1,2
.
Then, from (
7.37
), (
7.16
), (
7.36
) (
7.35
),
and a Riemann sum approximation, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{\lambda,N}^{(2)}&\lesssim\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
0<|n|<\frac{N}{4}\end{subarray}}|n|^{2-2\rho}\sup_{D_{2}(n)}|n_{1}|^{-2\rho}|n_{2}|^{-2\rho}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{-4\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
|m|\lesssim\lambda N\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{m}{\lambda}\Big|^{2-2\rho}\frac{1}{\lambda}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{-4\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\int_{\lambda^{-1}}^{N}x^{2-2\rho}dx\\
&\lesssim K(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{aligned}

(7.40)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
where
K(\lambda,N)
is as in (
7.33
).
Here, the last step follows
from separately estimating
the cases when
\rho<\frac{3}{2}
,

\rho=\frac{3}{2}
,
and
\rho>\frac{3}{2}
.
This proves
(
7.32
) in this case.

\bullet

Case 2:
on
D_{3}
.

In this case, we have
m_{s,N}(n_{2})=1

and
|n|\sim|n_{1}|\gtrsim N\gtrsim|n_{2}|
.
Then, from (
7.1
) and the mean value theorem, we have

\displaystyle M_{N}(\bar{n})=|m_{s,N}(n)-m_{s,N}(n_{1})|\lesssim\frac{|n|^{s-1}|n_{2}|}{N^{s}}.

(7.41)

Proceeding as in (
7.40
) with
(
7.38
), (
7.16
), (
7.41
),
and a Riemann sum approximation, we have

\displaystyle A_{\lambda,N}^{(3)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|n|\geq\frac{N}{4}}|n|^{2s-2\rho}\sum_{D_{3}(n)}\frac{1}{|n_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}|n_{2}|^{2\rho-2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{-4\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
|m_{2}|\lesssim\lambda N\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{m_{2}}{\lambda}\Big|^{2-2\rho}\frac{1}{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim K(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
thus yielding (
7.32
).

\bullet

Case 3:
on
D_{4}
.

From (
7.35
), we have

\displaystyle|M_{N}(\bar{n})|\leq|m_{s,N}(n)|+|m_{s,N}(n_{1})m_{s,N}(n_{2})|.

(7.42)

Then, from (
7.39
) with (
7.16
) and (
7.42
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{\lambda,N}^{(4)}&\lesssim\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{4s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\hphantom{XX}\times\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(n)}\cdot|n|^{2-2\rho}\frac{|m_{s,N}(n)|^{2}}{|n_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}|n_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}\\
&\quad+\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\hphantom{XX}\times\sup_{n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(n)}\cdot|n|^{2-2\rho}|n_{1}|^{-2\rho}|n_{2}|^{-2\rho}\\
&=:\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}.\end{split}

(7.43)

Let us first estimate
I
.

\circ

Subcase 3.a:

|n|\ll N
.

In this case, we have
m_{s,N}(n)=1
.
Then, proceeding as in (
7.40
) with
|n_{1}|\sim|n_{2}|\gtrsim N
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt&\lesssim\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{-4\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n|\ll N\end{subarray}}|n|^{2-2\rho}\\
&\lesssim K(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{split}

(7.44)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
thus yielding (
7.32
).

\circ

Subcase 3.b:

|n|\gtrsim N
.

In this case, we have
|m_{N}(n)|\lesssim N^{-s}|n|^{s}
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
|n|\lesssim|n_{1}|
.
We note that, for
\rho\geq\frac{3}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{3-3\rho-3\gamma}N^{\frac{3}{2}-3\rho}\leq\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-2\rho-3\gamma}N^{-2\rho}.

(7.45)

Then, from (
7.43
), a Riemann sum approximation,
Lemma
2.1
,
and
(
7.45
), we have

I

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{2s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n|,|n-n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n\rangle^{4\rho-2}\langle n-n_{1}\rangle^{2s+2\rho}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{2s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}\\
|\frac{m}{\lambda}|,|\frac{m}{\lambda}-n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle\frac{m}{\lambda}\rangle^{4\rho-2}\langle\frac{m}{\lambda}-n_{1}\rangle^{2s+2\rho}}\frac{1}{\lambda}

(7.46)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{2s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\intt_{|\xi|,|\xi-n_{1}|\gtrsim N}\frac{d\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle^{4\rho-2}\langle\xi-n_{1}\rangle^{2s+2\rho}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{3-6\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim K(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma},

provided that
(
4.11
) holds.
Here, in applying Lemma
2.1
,
we used

\displaystyle\begin{split}\intt_{|\xi|,|\xi-n_{1}|\gtrsim N}\frac{d\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle^{4\rho-2}\langle\xi-n_{1}\rangle^{2s+2\rho}}&\lesssim N^{2-2s-6\rho+\alpha+\beta}\intt_{|\xi|,|\xi-n_{1}|\gtrsim N}\frac{d\xi}{\langle\xi\rangle^{\alpha}\langle\xi-n_{1}\rangle^{\beta}}\\
&\lesssim N^{3-2s-6\rho}\end{split}

(7.47)

by taking some
0\leq\alpha,\beta<1

such that
\alpha\leq 4\rho-2

and
\beta\leq 2s+2\rho

satisfying
\alpha+\beta>1
.

Next, we estimate
I I
.

\circ

Subcase 3.c:

|n|\ll N
.

By substituting
|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\gtrsim N
in (
7.43
),
we see that (
7.44
) also holds for
I I
in this case.

\circ

Subcase 3.d:

|n|\gtrsim N
.

Without loss of generality, assume that
N\lesssim|n|\lesssim|n_{1}|
.
From (
7.43
),
a Riemann sum approximation,
Lemma
2.1
,
and
(
7.45
),
we have

I I

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{5-6\rho-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\lambda}^{*}\\
|n|,|n-n_{1}|\gtrsim N\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\langle n\rangle^{4\rho-2}\langle n-n_{1}\rangle^{2\rho}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{3-6\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim K(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma},

provided that
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,
where, at the second inequality, we proceeded as in (
7.47
).
∎

7.4.
Global well-posedness on the circle

We are now ready to put together all the ingredients from the previous subsections
and prove
global well-posedness
of the periodic modulated KdV (
1.2
) in negative Sobolev spaces
as claimed in Theorem
1.3
(ii).

Fix
u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
for some
s<0
(to be determined later).
Let

u
be a solution to the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on
\mathbb{T}

with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}

whose existence for short times is guaranteed by Theorem
1.3
(i).
In view of the blowup alternative (
3.50
),
our goal is to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u(t)\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\leq C(T)<\infty

(7.48)

for each finite
T>0
.
In view of
(
7.3
), (
7.4
), (
7.10
), and (
7.28
),
the bound (
7.48
) follows once we prove the following bound on the modified energy:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq\lambda^{3}T}\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\leq C(T,\lambda)<\infty

for some
\lambda\geq 1
.

From (
7.3
) and (
7.10
), we have

\|Iu_{0}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\lesssim\lambda^{-s-\frac{3}{2}}N^{-s}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}.

(7.49)

Then, given small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
we can choose

\lambda\sim N^{-\frac{2s}{3+2s}},

(7.50)

such that

\displaystyle\|Iu_{0}^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\leq\varepsilon_{0},

(7.51)

provided that
s>-\frac{3}{2}
.
Here,
the implicit constant in (
7.50
) depends only on
{\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{T})}}
.

We now study the increment of the modified energy.
Let us first make preliminary analysis
on the remainder term

R^{\lambda}_{t,r}
defined in (
7.31
).
By rewriting (
7.30
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}R^{\lambda}_{t,r}&=\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}-\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&\quad-\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\com_{t,r}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(r))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})},\end{split}

(7.52)

where

\com_{t,r}
is as in
(
7.31
).
Then,
from (
2.2
) and (
7.52
)
with

\com_{t_{1},t_{3}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}))-\com_{t_{2},t_{3}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}))=\com_{t_{1},t_{2}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3})),

which follows from (
7.31
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\updelta R^{\lambda})_{t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}}&=R^{\lambda}_{t_{1},t_{3}}-R^{\lambda}_{t_{1},t_{2}}-R^{\lambda}_{t_{2},t_{3}}\\
&=\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{2})-I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\com_{t_{1},t_{2}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{2}),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{2}))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&\quad+\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\com_{t_{1},t_{2}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{2})-\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{2}))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\\
&\quad+\big\langle I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\com_{t_{1},t_{2}}(\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}),\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{2})-\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{3}))\big\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}\end{aligned}

(7.53)

for any
t_{1}>t_{2}>t_{3}\geq 0
.

Fix an interval
J\subset[0,\lambda^{3}T]

and
0<\alpha<\gamma
such that
\alpha+\gamma>1
.
Then, it follows from (
7.53
), Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality,
Proposition
7.6
,
and (
7.27
)
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |(\updelta R^{\lambda})_{t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}}|&\lesssim K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\gamma}|t_{2}-t_{3}|^{\alpha}\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}\|^{3}_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}(J;L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}\end{aligned}

for any

t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq t_{3}\geq 0

belonging to the interval
J
,
where
K(\lambda,N)
is as in (
7.33
).
Since
\alpha+\gamma>1
,
it follows from the sewing lemma
(Lemma
3.3
)
that

\displaystyle|R^{\lambda}_{t_{1},t_{2}}|\lesssim K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\alpha+\gamma}\|\mathcal{I}\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}\|^{3}_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}(J;L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}

(7.54)

for any

t_{1}>t_{2}\geq 0

belonging to the interval
J
.

We now put everything together.
Let
\tau\sim 1
be the local existence time
for the scaled modulated
I
-KdV (
7.20
)
given by (
7.26
) in Corollary
7.5
,
corresponding to initial data
v_{0}
with

\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}^{2}=2\varepsilon_{0}
.
Namely, if we have

\displaystyle\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}

(7.55)

for some

0\leq t_{0}\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
then Corollary
7.5

guarantees that the solution
Iu^{\lambda}
to the scaled modulated
I
-KdV (
7.20
) exists on
the time interval
[t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]\cap[0,\lambda^{3}T]
, satisfying the bound:

\displaystyle\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau];L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda}))}^{2}\leq C_{\alpha}\varepsilon_{0}.

(7.56)

for some
C_{\alpha}>0
.
In particular, in view of (
7.51
) (which states that (
7.55
) is satisfied with
t_{0}=0
),
we see that
the solution
Iu^{\lambda}
exists on
[0,\tau]
.

Suppose now that by choosing
N\gg 1

(and thus
\lambda=\lambda(N)\gg 1

in view of (
7.50
)),
we can make

K(\lambda,N)
in (
7.33
)
as small as we need;
see (
7.61
) and (
7.62
).
Then,
from
(
7.30
), (
7.51
),
Proposition
7.6
,
(
7.54
),
and (
7.56
) with
t_{0}=0
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq\tau}\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}&\leq\varepsilon_{0}+C_{2}K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}\\
&\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}.\end{split}

(7.57)

In particular, the solution
Iu^{\lambda}
exists on
[\tau,2\tau]
,
satisfying
(
7.55
) and (
7.56
)
with
t_{0}=\tau
.
Then,
from
(
7.30
), (
7.57
)
Proposition
7.6
,
(
7.54
), and (
7.56
) with
t_{0}=\tau
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{\tau\leq t\leq 2\tau}\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}&\leq\varepsilon_{0}+2C_{2}K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}\\
&\leq 2\varepsilon_{0},\end{split}

(7.58)

provided that
we can make

K(\lambda,N)
small by
choosing
N\gg 1

(and thus
\lambda=\lambda(N)\gg 1
).
If (
7.58
) holds,
then
the solution
Iu^{\lambda}
exists on
[2\tau,3\tau]
,
satisfying
(
7.55
) and (
7.56
)
with
t_{0}=2\tau
,
which allows us to iterate this process.

After
j
steps,
this iterative process shows that
the solution
Iu^{\lambda}
exists on
[0,(j+1)\tau]
,
satisfying (
7.55
) and (
7.56
)
with
t_{0}=j\tau
, and moreover, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq j\tau}\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{0}+jC_{2}K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}.

(7.59)

Then, proceeding as in (
7.58
) with (
7.59
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sup_{0\leq t\leq(j+1)\tau}\|I\mathbf{u}^{\lambda}(t)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{\lambda})}&\leq\varepsilon_{0}+(j+1)C_{2}K(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}\\
&\leq 2\varepsilon_{0},\end{split}

(7.60)

provided that
we can make

K(\lambda,N)
small by
choosing
N\gg 1

(and thus
\lambda=\lambda(N)\gg 1
).

Our goal is to construct the solution
Iu^{\lambda}
on the time interval
[0,\lambda^{3}T]
,
for which we need to iterate this process
j\sim\lambda^{3}T
times,
since
\tau\sim 1
.
From (
7.60
),
we see that
it is possible
to reach the target time
\lambda^{3}T

(such that
\lambda^{3}T
is (at most) the doubling time
of the modified energy),
provided that

\displaystyle\lambda^{3}T\lesssim K(\lambda,N)^{-1}=\begin{cases}\lambda^{-3+3\rho+3\gamma}N^{-\frac{3}{2}+3\rho},&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<\rho<\frac{3}{2}$},\\
\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}+3\gamma}N^{3}(\log N+\log\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}},&\text{if $\rho=\frac{3}{2}$},\\
\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}+2\rho+3\gamma}N^{2\rho},&\text{if $\rho>\frac{3}{2}$}.\end{cases}

(7.61)

In view of (
7.50
),
we can guarantee (
7.61
) by choosing
N\gg 1
,
provided that

\displaystyle s>\begin{cases}\frac{3-6\rho}{6-4\gamma},&\text{if }\frac{1}{2}<\rho<\frac{3}{2},\\
\frac{-2\rho}{3-2\gamma},&\text{if }\rho\geq\frac{3}{2}.\end{cases}

(7.62)

Therefore, noting that

\frac{-2\rho}{3-2\gamma}<-\frac{3}{2}
for

\rho\geq\frac{3}{2}
and

\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
,
we conclude
from (
1.9
) and (
7.62
) with
s>-\frac{3}{2}

that
global well-posedness of the modulated KdV in
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
,
provided that

\rho>\frac{1}{2}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.
satisfy (
1.10
).
This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.3
(ii).

7.5.
Real line case

We conclude this section by discussing the real line case.
In this case, we need to work with the non-homogeneous Sobolev spaces whose
scaling property is worse
(for example, compare (
7.16
) and (
7.64
))
and thus we need to proceed with care.
In particular, we overcome the issue of the worse scaling properties
by changes of variables
(which are not available in the periodic case).
While we used the same notations from Subsections
7.1
-
7.4
,
all the objects
(equations, functions, and operators) are
to be understood as those defined on the real line.

Let
s<0
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\|u_{0}^{\lambda}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-s}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})},

(7.63)

where
u_{0}^{\lambda}
is as in (
7.6
).
As in the periodic case,
our goal is to study the scaled modulated
I
-KdV (
7.20
)
on the real line.
Given small
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, it follows from
(
7.3
) (which also holds true on
\mathbb{R}
)
and (
7.63
) that

\|Iu_{0}^{\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\lesssim\lambda^{-s-\frac{3}{2}}N^{-s}\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\varepsilon_{0},

where the second step
follows from choosing

\lambda\sim N^{-\frac{2s}{3+2s}}
as in (
7.50
),
provided that
s>-\frac{3}{2}
.
This allows us to reduce the study to the small data regime
as in the periodic case.

Given
\lambda\geq 1
, let
X^{\lambda}
be the driver defined in (
7.13
),
where we view all the operators and functions
interpreted as those defined on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then, we have the following
analogue of Lemma
7.1

on the regularity of the driver
X^{\lambda}

whose proof follows
from a slight modification of the proof of
Proposition
4.4
(i).

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
,
and let
X^{\lambda}
be as in (
7.13
).
Suppose that
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
and
s<0

satisfy
(
1.11
).
Then,
the driver
X^{\lambda}

belongs to
\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{R})
defined in (
2.11
),
satisfying the bound
:

\displaystyle\|X^{\lambda}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}))}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
.

Once we prove Lemma
7.7
,
an analogue of Lemma
7.3

on equivalence of the
unscaled and scaled problems in the current real line case follows
from the same proof.

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

Proceeding as in
(
7.16
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))&=\bigg|\int_{r}^{t}e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi})w^{\lambda}(t^{\prime})}dt^{\prime}\bigg|=\lambda^{3}\bigg|\int_{\lambda^{-3}r}^{\lambda^{-3}t}e^{i\lambda^{3}\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi})w(t^{\prime})}dt^{\prime}\bigg|\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}\langle\lambda^{3}\xi\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\rangle^{-\rho}\end{aligned}

(7.64)

for any
\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
\xi=\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}
.

Proceeding as in (
4.29
),
(
4.31
), and (
4.33
), we have

\displaystyle\|X_{t,r}^{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}))}
