\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|\xi|<1}\intt_{|\xi_{1}|<2}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\xi^{2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}}{\langle\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle\xi-\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}}d\xi_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{|\xi_{1}|\geq 2}\intt_{|\xi|<1}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\xi^{2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}}{\langle\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle\xi-\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}}d\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{|\xi_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\intt_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\xi|\geq 1\\
|\xi-\xi_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\end{subarray}}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\xi^{2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}}{\langle\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle\xi-\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}}d\xi

(7.65)

\displaystyle\quad+2\sup_{|\xi|\geq 1}\intt_{|\xi-\xi_{1}|<\frac{1}{2}}\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\xi^{2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}}{\langle\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}\langle\xi-\xi_{1}\rangle^{2s}}d\xi_{1}

\displaystyle=:A^{\lambda}_{1}+A^{\lambda}_{2}+A^{\lambda}_{3}+A^{\lambda}_{4}.

We first estimate
A^{\lambda}_{1}
.
In this case, we have
|\xi|,|\xi_{1}|,|\xi-\xi_{1}|\lesssim 1
.
Without loss of generality,
assume that
|\xi|\lesssim|\xi-\xi_{1}|
.
Then, from (
7.64
)
and a change of variable
\zeta_{1}=\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}\xi_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{\lambda}_{1}&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi|<1}\xi^{2}\intt_{|\xi_{1}|<2}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}\xi_{1}\rangle^{2\rho}}d\xi_{1}\\
&\leq\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi|<1}\intt_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\langle\zeta_{1}\rangle^{2\rho}}d\zeta_{1}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{split}

(7.66)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
provided that
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
.

From (
7.64
),
a change of variable
\zeta=\lambda\xi
,
and relabelling
\zeta_{1}=\lambda\xi_{1}
,
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{\lambda}_{2}&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\quad\times\sup_{|\xi_{1}|\geq 2}\intt_{|\xi|<1}\xi^{2}\frac{1}{|\xi_{1}(\xi-\xi_{1})|^{2s}\langle\lambda^{3}\xi\xi_{1}(\xi-\xi_{1})\rangle^{2\rho}}d\xi\\
&=\lambda^{3+4s-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\quad\times\sup_{|\zeta_{1}|\geq 2\lambda}\intt_{|\zeta|<\lambda}\zeta^{2}\frac{1}{|\zeta_{1}(\zeta-\zeta_{1})|^{2s}\langle\zeta\zeta_{1}(\zeta-\zeta_{1})\rangle^{2\rho}}d\zeta\end{split}

(7.67)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
.
Denote by
A_{2}^{\lambda,(1)}
, denoted by
the contribution to
A^{\lambda}_{2}
from
|\zeta|\lesssim 1
.
Then,
proceeding as in (
4.37
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{\lambda,(1)}_{2}&\lesssim\lambda^{3+4s-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{split}

(7.68)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
provided that

\displaystyle\rho>0,\quad-\frac{3}{2}<s<0,\quad\text{and}\quad s\geq-\rho.

Let
A_{2}^{\lambda,(2)}=A_{2}^{\lambda}-A_{2}^{\lambda,(1)}
.
Using
|\zeta_{1}|\sim|\zeta-\zeta_{1}|\gtrsim\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}A^{\lambda,(2)}_{2}&\lesssim\lambda^{3+4s-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\zeta_{1}|\geq 2\lambda}\intt_{1\ll|\zeta|<\lambda}|\zeta|^{2-2\rho}\frac{1}{|\zeta_{1}(\zeta-\zeta_{1})|^{2s+2\rho}}d\zeta\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-4\rho-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\zeta_{1}|\geq 2\lambda}\intt_{1\ll|\zeta|<\lambda}|\zeta|^{2-2\rho}d\zeta\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}^{2}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{split}

(7.69)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
provided that
s+\rho\geq 0

and
\rho\geq\frac{1}{2}
.

As for
A^{\lambda}_{3}
,
there is no low frequency issue.
Thus, by
proceeding as in (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) with (
7.64
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{3}^{\lambda}&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{2}}\intt_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\xi|\geq 1\\
|\xi-\xi_{1}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\xi|^{2s+2-2\rho}}{|\xi_{1}(\xi-\xi_{1})|^{2s+2\rho}}d\xi\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{aligned}

(7.70)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
.
provided that
(
4.11
) holds.

As for
A_{4}^{\lambda}
,
we first note that

|\xi_{1}|\sim|\xi|\geq 1

and
\langle\xi-\xi_{1}\rangle\sim 1

under

|\xi|\geq 1
and
|\xi-\xi_{1}|<\frac{1}{2}
.
Then, from (
7.64
), (
4.28
), and a change of variables

\zeta_{2}=\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}(\xi-\xi_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{4}^{\lambda}&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi|\geq 1}\xi^{2}\intt_{|\xi-\xi_{1}|<\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}(\xi-\xi_{1})\rangle^{2\rho}}d\xi_{1}\\
&=\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\intt_{|\zeta_{2}|<\frac{\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}}{2}}\frac{1}{\langle\zeta_{2}\rangle^{2\rho}}d\zeta_{2}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{aligned}

(7.71)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
, provided that
\rho>\frac{1}{2}

(and any
s\in\mathbb{R}
).

Therefore, putting (
4.33
),
(
7.65
), (
7.66
),
(
7.67
), (
7.68
),
(
7.69
),
(
7.70
),
and (
7.71
)
together, we conclude
from Lemma
3.1
(i)
that

X^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{R})
under

s<0
and (
1.11
).
∎

We now consider the
scaled modulated
I
-KdV (
7.20
)
on
\mathbb{R}
.
Let
Y^{\lambda}
be the associated
driver
defined in (
7.21
),
where we view all the operators and functions
interpreted as those defined on
\mathbb{R}
.
Then,
as a corollary to
Lemma
7.7
, we obtain the following
regularity property
of the driver
Y^{\lambda}

and local well-posedness of
the scaled modulated
I
-KdV (
7.20
)
on the real line.
See
Lemma
7.4

and Corollary
7.5

for the corresponding statements
in the periodic setting
studied earlier.

Corollary 7.8
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
,
and let
X^{\lambda}
be as in (
7.13
).
Suppose that
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
and
s<0

satisfy
(
1.11
).
Then,
the driver
Y^{\lambda}
in (
7.21
)
belongs to
\mathcal{X}^{0,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{R})
defined in (
2.9
),
satisfying the bound
:

\displaystyle\|Y^{\lambda}_{t,r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}

(7.72)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
.

Moreover, given any

v_{0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
t_{0}\in[0,\lambda^{3}T]
,
there exist
C_{0}>0
,
\theta>0
, both independent of
v_{0}
and
t_{0}
,
and a unique solution
Iu

to
the scaled modulated
I
-KdV equation (
7.20
)
on the real line
with
Iu^{\lambda}(t_{0})=v_{0}

on the time interval
[t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]\cap[0,\lambda^{3}T]
,
belonging to the class
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{w}^{0}\big(([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau]\cap[0,\lambda^{3}T])\times\mathbb{R}\big),

where the local existence time

\tau=\tau\big(\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\big)>0
satisfies

\displaystyle\tau\geq C_{0}\|Y^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{0,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{R})}^{-\theta}\big(1+\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\big)^{-\theta}.

Moreover, given any
0<\alpha<\gamma
,
there exists
C_{\alpha}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|Iu^{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([t_{0},t_{0}+\tau];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))}\leq C_{\alpha}\|v_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

Proof.

The bound (
7.72
)
follows form Lemma
7.7
,
the interpolation lemma (see
[
32
, Lemma 12.1]
),
and
with

\displaystyle M(\bar{\xi})\lesssim\frac{\langle\xi\rangle^{s}}{\langle\xi_{1}\rangle^{s}\langle\xi_{2}\rangle^{s}}

for any

\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R}

with
\xi=\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}
,
where
M(\bar{\xi})=M(\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2})

is as in (
7.24
).
Then, from
Lemma
3.1
(i),
we conclude that

Y^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{X}^{0,\gamma}_{2}([0,\lambda^{3}T]\times\mathbb{R})
.

By noting that

\lambda^{\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}\leq 1

for any
\lambda\geq 1
,
the local well-posedness claim
follows
from the regularity of the driver
Y^{\lambda}
and Proposition
3.8
.
We omit details.
∎

Next, we present a key
commutator estimate.
While we proceed as in the proof of
Proposition
7.6
,
we provide details
since it involves changes of variables,
unique to the current real line setting.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
\lambda\geq 1
.
Given
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular
on
[0,T]

in the sense of Definition
1.1
,
and let
X^{\lambda}
be as in (
7.13
).
Fix
s<0
satisfying (
1.9
).
Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\com_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}&=\|IX^{\lambda}_{t,r}(f_{1},f_{2})-X^{\lambda}_{t,r}(If_{1},If_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}}\\
&\lesssim\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)\|\Phi^{w}\|_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{\gamma}\|If_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|If_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\end{aligned}

(7.73)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
\lambda\geq 1
, and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
where
\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)
is
given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)=\begin{cases}\lambda^{3-3\rho-3\gamma}N^{\frac{3}{2}-3\rho},&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<\rho<\frac{3}{2}$},\\
\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}N^{-3}\sqrt{\log N+\log\lambda},&\text{if $\rho=\frac{3}{2}$},\\
\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}N^{-3},&\text{if $\rho>\frac{3}{2}$}.\end{cases}

(7.74)

Proof.

Proceeding as in
the proof of Proposition
7.6
,
we have

LHS of (
7.73
)

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\sum_{j=2}^{4}B_{\lambda,N}^{(j)}\|If_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\|If_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
B_{\lambda,N}^{(j)}
,
j=2,3,4
, is given by

\displaystyle B_{\lambda,N}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\intt_{|\xi|<\frac{N}{4}}|\xi|^{2}\sup_{D_{2}(\xi)}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}|M_{N}(\bar{\xi})|^{2}}{|m_{s,N}(\xi_{1})|^{2}|m_{s,N}(\xi_{2})|^{2}}d\xi,

(7.75)

\displaystyle B_{\lambda,N}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{N}{4}}|\xi|^{2}\int_{D_{3}(\xi)}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}|M_{N}(\bar{\xi})|^{2}}{|m_{s,N}(\xi_{1})|^{2}|m_{s,N}(\xi_{2})|^{2}}d\xi_{1},

(7.76)

\displaystyle B_{\lambda,N}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(\xi)}\cdot|\xi|^{2}\frac{|\Phi^{w^{\lambda}}_{t,r}(\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{\xi}))|^{2}|M_{N}(\bar{\xi})|^{2}}{|m_{s,N}(\xi_{1})|^{2}|m_{s,N}(\xi_{2})|^{2}}d\xi.

(7.77)

Here,

M_{N}(\bar{\xi})=M_{N}(\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2})

and
D_{j}(\xi)
,
j=2,3,4
,
are
as in (
7.35
) and (
7.36
), respectively,
with obvious modifications
to the current real line setting.

\bullet

Case 1:
on
D_{2}
.

In this case, we have
|\xi_{1}|,|\xi_{2}|\sim N

and
|m_{N}(\xi)|\sim|m_{N}(\xi_{j})|\sim 1
,
j=1,2
.
Then, from (
7.75
), (
7.64
),
and
a change of variables
\zeta=\lambda^{3}N^{2}\xi
, we have

\displaystyle B_{\lambda,N}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\intt_{|\xi|\leq\frac{N}{4}}\frac{\xi^{2}}{\langle\lambda^{3}N^{2}\xi\rangle^{2\rho}}d\xi

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-3-6\gamma}N^{-6}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\intt_{|\zeta|\lesssim\lambda^{3}N^{3}}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{\langle\zeta\rangle^{2\rho}}d\zeta

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
where
\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)
is as in (
7.74
).
This proves
(
7.73
) in this case.

\bullet

Case 2:
on
D_{3}
.

In this case, we have
|\xi|\sim|\xi_{1}|\gtrsim N\gtrsim|\xi_{2}|
.
Then, from (
7.76
), (
7.64
),
(
7.41
),
and
a change of variables
\zeta_{2}=\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}\xi_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{\lambda,N}^{(3)}&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{N}{4}}\int_{D_{3}(\xi)}\frac{|\xi_{2}|^{2}}{\langle\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}\xi_{2}\rangle^{2\rho}}\ d\xi_{2}\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{-3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}^{\rho,\gamma}_{T}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi|\geq\frac{N}{4}}\xi^{-6}\int_{|\zeta_{2}|\lesssim\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}N}\frac{|\zeta_{2}|^{2}}{\langle\zeta_{2}\rangle^{2\rho}}\ d\zeta_{2}\\
&\lesssim\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\end{split}

(7.78)

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
thus yielding (
7.73
).

\bullet

Case 3:
on
D_{4}
.

From (
7.77
) with (
7.64
) and (
7.42
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{\lambda,N}^{(4)}&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\gamma}N^{4s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\hphantom{XX}\times\sup_{\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{|\xi|^{2}|m_{s,N}(\xi)|^{2}}{|\xi_{1}|^{2s}|\xi_{2}|^{2s}\langle\lambda^{3}\xi\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\rangle^{2\rho}}d\xi\\
&\quad+\lambda^{6-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(\xi)}\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{\langle\lambda^{3}\xi\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\rangle^{2\rho}}d\xi\\
&=:\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}.\end{split}

(7.79)

Let us first estimate
I
.

\circ

Subcase 3.a:

|\xi|\ll N
.

In this case, we have
m_{s,N}(\xi)=1

and
|\xi_{1}|\sim|\xi_{2}|\gtrsim N
.
Then, by
a change of variables
\zeta=\lambda^{3}\xi_{1}^{2}\xi
, we have

I

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-3-6\gamma}N^{4s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi_{1}|\gtrsim N}|\xi_{1}|^{-6-4s}\intt_{|\zeta|\lesssim\lambda^{3}\xi_{1}^{2}N}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{\langle\zeta\rangle^{2\rho}}d\zeta

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
thus yielding (
7.73
).
Here,
in estimating the integral,
we used the fact that
(i)
s+\rho\geq 0

when
\frac{1}{2}<\rho<\frac{3}{2}

and
(ii)
s>-\frac{3}{2}
when
\rho\geq\frac{3}{2}
.

\circ

Subcase 3.b:

|\xi|\gtrsim N
.

In this case, we have
|m_{N}(\xi)|\lesssim N^{-s}|\xi|^{s}
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
|\xi|\lesssim|\xi_{1}|
.
We note that, for
\rho\geq\frac{3}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda^{3-3\rho-3\gamma}N^{\frac{3}{2}-3\rho}\leq\lambda^{-\frac{3}{2}-3\gamma}N^{-3}.

(7.80)

Then,
proceeding as in (
7.46
)
with
(
7.79
),
(
7.47
),
and
(
7.80
) with (
7.74
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 1.42262pt\text{I}\hskip 1.42262pt&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{2s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\intt_{|\xi|\gtrsim N}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{|\xi|^{2+2s-2\rho}}{|\xi_{1}|^{2s+2\rho}|\xi_{2}|^{2s+2\rho}}d\xi\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{2s}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\intt_{|\xi|\gtrsim N}\mathbf{1}_{D_{4}(\xi)}\cdot\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle^{4\rho-2}\langle\xi_{2}\rangle^{2s+2\rho}}d\xi\\
&\lesssim\lambda^{6-6\rho-6\gamma}N^{3-6\rho}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\\
&\lesssim\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma},\end{split}

(7.81)

provided that
(
4.11
) holds.

Next, we estimate
I I
.

\circ

Subcase 3.c:

|\xi|\ll N
.

In this case, we have

|\xi_{1}|\sim|\xi_{2}|\gtrsim N
.
Then, proceeding as in (
7.78
)
with
a change of variables
\zeta=\lambda^{3}\xi_{1}^{2}\xi
, we have

I

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-3-6\gamma}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}\sup_{|\xi_{1}|\gtrsim N}|\xi_{1}|^{-6}\intt_{|\zeta|\lesssim\lambda^{3}\xi_{1}^{2}N}\frac{\zeta^{2}}{\langle\zeta\rangle^{2\rho}}d\zeta

\displaystyle\lesssim\widetilde{K}(\lambda,N)^{2}\|\Phi^{w}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{W}_{T}^{\rho,\gamma}}|t-r|^{2\gamma}

for any
0\leq r<t\leq\lambda^{3}T
,
thus yielding (
7.73
).

\circ

Subcase 3.d:

|\xi|\gtrsim N
.

Without loss of generality, assume that
N\lesssim|\xi|\lesssim|\xi_{1}|
.
Then, by noting
that
\langle\lambda^{3}\xi\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\rangle\sim\lambda^{3}|\xi\xi_{1}\xi_{2}|\gtrsim\lambda^{3}\xi^{2}|\xi_{2}|
,
we can repeat the computation in (
7.81
)
and obtain (
7.73
).
∎

We conclude this section by briefly discussing a proof of Theorem

1.7
(ii).
In view of
(
7.63
), Corollary
7.8
,
and Lemma
7.9

(compare (
7.74
) with (
7.33
)),
there is no difference from the periodic case, when
\frac{1}{2}<\rho\leq\frac{3}{2}
,
yielding
global well-posedness of the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on the real line
for the same range of
s
.
When
\rho>\frac{3}{2}
,
by substituting
\rho=\frac{3}{2}
in the last case of (
7.61
),
we obtain the condition
s>-\frac{3}{3-2\gamma}
(which is smaller than
-\frac{3}{2}
for
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
).
In view of the regularity restriction
s>-\frac{3}{2}

from the local well-posedness (see (
1.11
)),
we conclude that global well-posedness
holds for
s>-\frac{3}{2}
.
This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.7
(ii).

8.
Invariance of the white noise under the modulated KdV

In this section, we briefly discuss
invariance of the white noise under
the modulated KdV, BO, ILW, and scaled ILW equations on the circle
(Theorems
1.18
and
1.19
).
For simplicity, we restrict our attention to the case
of the modulated KdV (
1.2
) on
\mathbb{T}
.
As mentioned in Subsection
1.5
,
Theorem
1.18
follows once we prove the following proposition.

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
. The white noise
\mu
defined in (
1.28
)
is invariant under the
the truncated modulated KdV dynamics (
1.23
).

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
set

\displaystyle u_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}u^{N}\qquad\text{and}\qquad u_{N}^{\perp}=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}u^{N},

where
\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}=\Id-\mathbf{P}_{N}
.
Then, the truncated modulated KdV (
1.23
)
decouples into the low frequency nonlinear but finite-dimensional dynamics,
satisfied by
u_{N}
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u_{N}+\partial_{x}^{3}u_{N}\cdot\partial_{t}w=\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{N}(u_{N}^{2})\\
u_{N}(0)=\mathbf{P}_{N}u^{N}(0)\end{cases}

(8.1)

and the high frequency linear dynamics,
satisfied by
u_{N}^{\perp}
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u_{N}^{\perp}+\partial_{x}^{3}u_{N}^{\perp}\cdot\partial_{t}w=0\\
u_{N}^{\perp}(0)=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}u^{N}(0).\end{cases}

(8.2)

Let
\rho>\frac{3}{4}
.
Then, by a slight modification of the proof of Theorem
1.3

applied to the low frequency part (
8.1
)
along with the conservation of the (truncated)
L^{2}
-norm,
we see that
the truncated modulated KdV (
1.23
)
is globally well-posed in
H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
since the high frequency dynamics is linear
(which is globally well-posed in
\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}H^{s}(\mathbb{T})
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
).

Let us write the white noise
\mu
as

\displaystyle\mu=\mu^{N,\textup{low}}\otimes\mu^{N,\textup{high}},

(8.3)

where
\mu^{N,\textup{low}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N})_{*}\mu

and
\mu^{N,\textup{high}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp})_{*}\mu

are the pushforward image
measures under the maps
\mathbf{P}_{N}
and
\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}
,
respectively.
From the rotational invariance of complex-valued Gaussian random variables, we see that

\mu^{N,\textup{high}}
is invariant
under the high frequency linear dynamics (
8.2
)
whose action on each Fourier coefficient is given by

\mathcal{F}(u_{N}^{\perp})(0,n)=g_{n}\mapsto\mathcal{F}(u_{N}^{\perp})(t,n)=e^{in^{3}w(t)}g_{n}
.

By Plancherel’s theorem with
\widehat{u}(-n)=\overline{\widehat{u}(n)}
,
we can write the truncated white noise
\mu^{N,\textup{low}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N})_{*}\mu
as

\displaystyle d\mu^{N,\textup{low}}

\displaystyle=Z_{N}^{-1}\exp\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{2}dx_{\mathbb{T}}\bigg)d\mathbf{P}_{N}u

\displaystyle=Z_{N}^{-1}\exp\bigg(-\sum_{n=1}^{N}|\widehat{u}(n)|^{2}\bigg)\prod_{n=1}^{N}d\widehat{u}(n),

where
d\widehat{u}(n)
,
n=1,\dots,N
, is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{C}\cong\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Arguing as in
Proposition
6.1
,
we see that the truncated
L^{2}
-norm

\|\mathbf{P}_{N}u\|_{L^{2}}

(= the
\mathbb{C}^{N}
-Euclidean distance on the Fourier side)
is conserved under the flow of (
8.1
).
Hence, invariance of the truncated white noise

\mu^{N,\textup{low}}
follows once we prove invariance of the Lebesgue measure

\prod_{n=1}^{N}d\widehat{u}(n)
.

Very formally, we can write the (time-dependent) Hamiltonian for (
8.1
)
as

\displaystyle H_{N}(u)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{2}\partial_{t}w(t)dx_{\mathbb{T}}+\frac{1}{3}\int_{\mathbb{T}}(\mathbf{P}_{N}u)^{3}dx_{\mathbb{T}}.

Unfortunately, the expression above does not make sense
since
\partial_{t}w(t)
is not well defined for fixed
t
.
Similarly, by viewing (
8.1
) on the Fourier side,
one may try to verify invariance of the Lebesgue measure

\prod_{n=1}^{N}d\widehat{u}(n)
by directly computing
the divergence of the vector field; see, for example,

[
105
, Lemma 4.1]
.
However, the divergence involves
\partial_{t}w(t)

and hence this approach fails as well.
(If we pretend that
\partial_{t}w(t)
makes sense for fixed
t
,
the divergence is indeed
0
since the terms
with
\partial_{t}w(t)
cancel out.)

A correct approach is to study the equations satisfied
by the modulated interaction representation.
Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
By
applying
U^{w}(t)^{-1}
to the truncated modulated KdV (
1.23
),
we obtain the following
truncated version of (
4.1
):

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}^{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)\mathbf{u}^{N})^{2}\big).

(8.4)

Since the action of
U^{w}(t)^{-1}
on each Fourier coefficient
is the multiplication by
e^{-in^{3}w(t)}
,
we see that the white noise is invariant under
U^{w}(t)^{-1}
,
namely

\displaystyle\mu=(U^{w}(t)^{-1})_{*}\mu.

See also Section 4 in
[
117
]
.
Thus, Proposition
8.1

follows once we prove invariance of the white noise
\mu

under (
8.4
).

As before, we decouple the truncated modulated KdV dynamics (
8.4
)
into
the finite-dimensional nonlinear dynamics for the low frequency part

\mathbf{u}_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{u}^{N}
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t)\mathbf{u}_{N})^{2}\big)\\
\mathbf{u}_{N}|_{t=0}=\mathbf{P}_{N}u_{0}^{\omega},\end{cases}

(8.5)

and the constant dynamics for the high frequency part

\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\perp}=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}\mathbf{u}^{N}
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\perp}=0\\
\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\perp}|_{t=0}=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}u_{0}^{\omega}.\end{cases}

(8.6)

Thanks to (
8.3
),
invariance of the
white noise
\mu=\mu^{N,\textup{low}}\otimes\mu^{N,\textup{high}}

under (
8.4
)
follows once we prove invariance
of
\mu^{N,\textup{low}}

(and
\mu^{N,\textup{high}}
) under (
8.5
)
(and (
8.6
), respectively).
Obviously,
\mu^{N,\textup{high}}
is invariant under
the constant dynamics (
8.5
).
In view of the
L^{2}
-conservation for (
8.5
)
(which follows from the
L^{2}
-conservation for (
8.1
)
and the
L^{2}
-unitarity of
U^{w}(t)^{-1}
),
invariance of the truncated white noise
\mu^{N,\textup{low}}

(and hence Proposition
8.1
and Theorem
1.18
)
follows
once we prove invariance of the Lebesgue measure

\prod_{n=1}^{N}d\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{N}(n)
under (
8.5
).

Lemma 8.2
.

The Lebesgue measure
\prod_{n=1}^{N}d\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{N}(n)
on
\mathbb{C}^{N}

is invariant under the truncated dynamics (
8.5
) viewed on the Fourier side.

Proof.

Let
p_{n}(t)=\Re\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{N}(t,n)

and
q_{n}(t)=\Im\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{N}(t,n)
for
1\leq|n|\leq N
.
Since
\mathbf{u}_{N}
is real-valued, we have

\displaystyle p_{-n}=p_{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad q_{-n}=-q_{n}.

(8.7)

Then, (
8.5
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}p_{n}=P_{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\partial_{t}q_{n}=Q_{n}

(8.8)

for
n=1,\dots N
,
where
P_{n}
and
Q_{n}
are defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{n}&=\frac{in}{2}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w(t)}(p_{n_{1}}+iq_{n_{1}})(p_{n_{2}}+iq_{n_{2}})\\
&\hphantom{XXX}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}e^{-i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w(t)}(p_{n_{1}}-iq_{n_{1}})(p_{n_{2}}-iq_{n_{2}})\bigg],\\
Q_{n}&=\frac{n}{2}\bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w(t)}(p_{n_{1}}+iq_{n_{1}})(p_{n_{2}}+iq_{n_{2}})\\
&\hphantom{XXX}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq|n_{1}|,|n_{2}|\leq N\\
n=n_{1}+n_{2}\end{subarray}}e^{-i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})w(t)}(p_{n_{1}}-iq_{n_{1}})(p_{n_{2}}-iq_{n_{2}})\bigg],\\
\end{split}

(8.9)

where
\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(\bar{n})
is as in (
4.6
).
From (
8.9
) with (
8.7
) (with
p_{0}=q_{0}=0
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{p_{n}}P_{n}&=in\Big(e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(n,-n,2n)w(t)}(p_{2n}+iq_{2n})\\
&\hphantom{XXl}-e^{-i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(n,-n,2n)w(t)}(p_{2n}-iq_{2n})\Big),\\
\partial_{q_{n}}Q_{n}&=n\Big(e^{i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(n,-n,2n)w(t)}(-i)(p_{2n}+iq_{2n})\\
&\hphantom{XXl}+e^{-i\Xi_{\text{\rm KdV}}(n,-n,2n)w(t)}i(p_{2n}-iq_{2n})\Big).\end{split}

(8.10)

Let
A(\bar{p},\bar{q})=A(p_{1},\dots,p_{N},q_{1},\dots,q_{N})

be the vector field of the dynamics (
8.8
) defined by

\displaystyle A(\bar{p},\bar{q})=(P_{1},\dots,P_{N},Q_{1},\dots,Q_{N}).

Then, from (
8.10
), we have

\displaystyle\text{div}_{\bar{p},\bar{q}}A(\bar{p},\bar{q})=\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\partial_{p_{n}}P_{n}+\partial_{q_{n}}Q_{n})=0.

Therefore
from Liouville’s theorem,
we conclude that
the Lebesgue measure
\prod_{n=1}^{N}d\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{N}(n)=\prod_{n=1}^{N}dp_{n}dq_{n}
is invariant under the truncated dynamics (
8.5
).
∎

9.
Stochastic modulated KdV

9.1.
Local well-posedness

In this subsection, we prove
local well-posedness of the stochastic modulated KdV (
1.30
)
(Theorem
1.21
(i)).

Let us first give a precise meaning of the stochastic term
\Psi
in (
1.38
).
Let
W
denote a cylindrical Wiener process on
L^{2}_{0}(\mathbb{T})
:

\displaystyle W(t)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}B_{n}(t)e_{n},

where

\{B_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}

is defined by

B_{n}(t)=\langle\xi,\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}\cdot e_{n}\rangle_{t,x}
.
Here,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{t,x}
denotes
the duality pairing on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}
.
As a result,
we see that
\{B_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}
is a family of mutually independent complex-valued
Brownian motions conditioned that
B_{-n}=\overline{B_{n}}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
.
Note that we have, for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
,

\text{Var}(B_{n}(t))=\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\xi,\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}\cdot e_{n}\rangle_{t,x}\overline{\langle\xi,\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}\cdot e_{n}\rangle_{t,x}}\big]=\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}\cdot e_{n}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}^{2}=t.

Then, we can write the stochastic term
\Psi
in (
1.38
)
as

\displaystyle\Psi(t)=\int_{0}^{t}U^{w}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\phi dW(t^{\prime})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}I_{t,0}(n)\phi(e_{n}),

(9.1)

where
I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(n)
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}
, is the Wiener integral given by

\displaystyle I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(n)=\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{-in^{3}w(t^{\prime})}dB_{n}(t^{\prime}).

(9.2)

Note that
I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(-n)=\overline{I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(n)}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Lemma 9.1
.

Given
\rho>0
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
Given
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
let
\phi\in\HS(L^{2}_{0}(\mathbb{T});H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T}))
.
Then,
given any
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
,

\Psi
defined in (
9.1
)
almost surely belongs to

\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
.

Proof.

By writing

\displaystyle\phi(e_{n})=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\phi_{nm}e_{m},\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*},

(9.3)

the Hilbert-Schmidt norm of
\phi
is given by

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{\HS(L^{2}_{0};H^{s}_{0})}=\bigg(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\|\phi(e_{n})\|_{H^{s}_{0}}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\bigg(\sum_{n,m\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\langle m\rangle^{2s}|\phi_{nm}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(9.4)

Then, from the equivalence of the
L^{p}(\Omega)
-norms
of a Gaussian random variable, (
9.1
),
(
9.3
),
the independence of

\{I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(n)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,
(
9.2
),
(
9.4
),
and the Wiener isometry, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\|\Psi(t_{1})-\Psi(t_{2})\|_{H^{s}}\big\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}&\sim_{p}\big\|\|\Psi(t_{1})-\Psi(t_{2})\|_{H^{s}}\big\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&=\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\langle m\rangle^{2s}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(n)\phi_{nm}\Big|^{2}\bigg]\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&=\bigg(\sum_{n,m\in\mathbb{Z}^{*}}\langle m\rangle^{2s}|\phi_{nm}|^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|I_{t_{1},t_{2}}(n)|^{2}\big]\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\sim\|\phi\|_{\HS(L^{2}_{0};H^{s}_{0})}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

(9.5)

for any
t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq 0
and any finite
p\geq 1
.
Then, given
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
,
by applying
the Kolmogorov continuity criterion (
[
2
, Theorem 2.1]
)
to (
9.5
)
with
p>\frac{2}{1-2\alpha}
,
we conclude that

\Psi\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([0,T];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
,
almost surely.
∎

We now present
a proof of Theorem
1.21
(i).

Proof of Theorem
1.21
(i).

Fix
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon
for some small
\varepsilon>0

such that
\alpha+\gamma>1
.
Define the map

\Gamma^{\Psi}=\Gamma^{\Psi}_{X,u_{0}}
on
\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([0,\tau];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))

by setting

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\Psi}(\mathbf{u})(t)

\displaystyle=u_{0}+\mathcal{I}^{X}(\mathbf{u})(t)+\Psi(t)

\displaystyle=\Gamma(\mathbf{u})+\Psi(t),

where
\Gamma(\mathbf{u})
is as in (
3.51
).
Then,
by setting
an almost surely finite radius
R=2\big(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}\big)

and choosing
an almost surely positive local existence time

\tau=\tau\big(R,\|X\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)}\big)=\tau\big(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}},\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{T}H^{s}_{x}},\|X\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)}\big)

sufficiently small,
it follows from (
3.53
) and (
3.63
) that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{\Psi}(\mathbf{u})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{T}H^{s}_{x}}+C\tau^{\gamma-\alpha}\|X\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)}(1+R)^{k-1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq R

and

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{\Psi}(\mathbf{u}^{1})-\Gamma^{\Psi}(\mathbf{u}^{2})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\tau^{\gamma-\alpha}\|X\|_{\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{k}(T)}(1+R)^{k-1}\|\mathbf{u}^{1}-\mathbf{u}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{u}^{1}-\mathbf{u}^{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau}H^{s}_{x}}

for any
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{1},\mathbf{u}^{2}\in B_{R}\subset\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([0,\tau];H^{s}(\mathcal{M}))
,
from which
we conclude
that
\Gamma^{\Psi}
is a contraction on
B_{R}
.
This proves
local well-posedness
of the stochastic modulated KdV (
1.30
)
on
\mathbb{T}
.
∎

9.2.
Global dynamics

In this subsection, we briefly
go over a proof of Theorem
1.21
(ii).
Since the argument is identical to that in
[
114
]
,
we only
sketch the main ideas.

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
consider the
the following truncated stochastic modulated KdV:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}u^{N}+\partial_{x}^{3}u^{N}\cdot\partial_{t}w=\partial_{x}\mathbf{P}_{N}\big((\mathbf{P}_{N}u^{N})^{2}\big)+\xi\\
u^{N}|_{t=0}=u_{0}^{\omega},\end{cases}

(9.6)

where
u_{0}^{\omega}
is the white noise given in (
1.29
).
In fact,
as in Section
8
,
it is more convenient to work at the level of the modulated interaction representation

\mathbf{u}(t)=U^{w}(t)^{-1}u(t)
.
Then, (
9.6
) becomes

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}^{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)\mathbf{u}^{N})^{2}\big)+U^{w}(t)^{-1}\xi\\
\mathbf{u}^{N}|_{t=0}=u_{0}^{\omega}.\end{cases}

(9.7)

As in Section
8
, the truncated dynamics (
9.7
)
decouples into
the finite-dimensional nonlinear dynamics for the low frequency part

\mathbf{u}_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{u}^{N}
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t)\mathbf{u}_{N})^{2}\big)+\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\xi\\
\mathbf{u}_{N}|_{t=0}=\mathbf{P}_{N}u_{0}^{\omega},\end{cases}

(9.8)

and the linear dynamics for the high frequency part

\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\perp}=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}\mathbf{u}^{N}
:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\perp}=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\xi\\
\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\perp}|_{t=0}=\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}u_{0}^{\omega}.\end{cases}

(9.9)

By a minor modification of
[
114
, Lemma 2.1]
,
we see that both (
9.8
) and (
9.9
)
are globally well-posed,
which in particular implies that (
9.7
) is globally well-posed.
For
t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq 0
,
we denote by

\Phi^{N,\textup{low}}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

and
\Phi^{N,\textup{high}}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

the solution maps for (
9.8
) and (
9.9
)
sending data
\varphi
at time
t_{2}
to the solutions

\Phi^{N,\textup{low}}_{t_{1},t_{2}}\varphi

and
\Phi^{N,\textup{high}}_{t_{1},t_{2}}\varphi
at time
t_{1}
.
We let
P^{N,\textup{low}}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

and
P^{N,\textup{high}}_{t_{1},t_{2}}
denote the transition semigroups
for (
9.8
) and (
9.9
), respectively,
defined as in (
1.32
), where the expectation
is taken over the noise restricted to the time interval
[t_{2},t_{1}]
.
We also use

\Phi^{N}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

and
P^{N}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

to denote the solution map and the transition semigroup for the truncated
stochastic modulated KdV (
9.7
).

Given
\sigma\geq 0
, let
\mu_{\sigma}
be the white noise measure (with variance
\sigma
)
as in (
1.31
).
Then, as in (
8.3
), we can write
\mu_{\sigma}
as

\displaystyle\mu_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\sigma}^{N,\textup{low}}\otimes\mu_{\sigma}^{N,\textup{high}},

where
\mu_{\sigma}^{N,\textup{low}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N})_{*}\mu_{\sigma}
and

\mu_{\sigma}^{N,\textup{high}}=(\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp})_{*}\mu_{\sigma}

are
the pushforward image measures of
\mu_{\sigma}
under

\mathbf{P}_{N}
and
\mathbf{P}_{N}^{\perp}
, respectively.
The high frequency dynamics (
9.8
)
is linear and it is easy to verify
that

\displaystyle(P^{N,\textup{high}}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{*}\mu^{N,\textup{high}}_{1+t_{2}}=\mu^{N,\textup{high}}_{1+t_{1}}.

(9.10)

See (
1.34
) (and (
1.33
))
for the definition of the adjoint
(P^{N,\textup{high}}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{*}
.
By writing it on the Fourier side,
we see that the low frequency dynamics (
9.8
)
is nothing but a finite-dimensional system of SDEs,
which can be viewed as the superposition of
the finite-dimensional modulated KdV dynamics:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((U^{w}(t)\mathbf{u}_{N})^{2}\big)

(9.11)

and the linear stochastic dynamics:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\xi.

(9.12)

As we saw in Section
8
,
the truncated white noise
\mu_{\sigma}^{N,\textup{low}}
(with any variance
\sigma
)
is invariant under the former dynamics (
9.11
),
while
the latter dynamics (
9.12
)
increases the variance of the white noise initial data by the length of the time interval under consideration.
Then, in view of the Lie-Trotter product formula (
1.40
),
we see that

\displaystyle(P^{N,\textup{low}}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{*}\mu^{N,\textup{low}}_{1+t_{2}}=\mu^{N,\textup{low}}_{1+t_{1}}.

(9.13)

Putting (
9.10
) and (
9.13
) together
invariance of the white noise
\mu_{\sigma}
(with any variance
\sigma
)
under
U^{w}(t)
,
we then obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, for any
t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle(P^{N}_{t_{1},t_{2}})^{*}\mu_{1+t_{2}}=\mu_{1+t_{1}},

where

P^{N}_{t_{1},t_{2}}

is the transition semigroup for the truncated dynamics (
9.7
).
Namely,
\{\mu_{1+t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}}

is an evolution system of measures
for the truncated dynamics (
9.7
)
and also for the truncated
stochastic modulated KdV equation (
9.6
).

In
[
114
]
,
the authors proved an analogous claim
for the truncated stochastic (unmodulated) KdV
by studying
the Kolmogorov forward equation
(= the Fokker-Planck equation)
for the distribution
of the Fourier coefficients
of a solution
u_{N}(t)
to the low frequency dynamics
and showing that the truncated white noise
is the unique solution to the
Kolmogorov forward equation;
see
[
114
, Section 2]
.
We point out that for such an argument,
we need to make use of the computations in the proof of Lemma
8.2
.
Namely, in the current modulated setting,
we need to work at the level of the modulated interaction representation.

Once we have
Proposition
9.2
at hand,
a variant of
Bourgain’s invariant measure argument
[
7
]

yields
the following
probabilistic uniform (in
N
) growth bound
on the solutions to the truncated dynamics (
9.7
).

Proposition 9.3
.

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathbf{u}^{N}
be the global solution to the truncated dynamics (
9.7
)
with the mean-zero white noise initial data
u_{0}^{\omega}
in (
1.29
).
Then,
given any
T\gg 1
and
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
, there exists a set
\Omega_{T,\varepsilon}(N)
such that

\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{T,\varepsilon}(N)^{c})<\varepsilon
and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathbf{u}^{N}(t)\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{T\log T}

(9.14)

on
\Omega_{T,\varepsilon}(N)
,
where the constant
C>0
is independent of
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
T\gg 1
, and
\varepsilon\ll 1
.

Proposition
9.3

follows from a straightforward modification of the proof of

[
114
, Proposition 4.1]
and thus we omit details.
The probabilistic uniform (in
N
) growth bound
(
9.14
)
together with the following lemma
on convergence of the Galerkin approximation
yields
the almost sure global well-posedness
claimed in
Theorem
1.21
(ii).
Finally, (
1.35
)
follows from Proposition
9.2

and
the following lemma
(with
\phi=\Id
).

Lemma 9.4

(Galerkin approximation)
.

Given
\rho>\frac{1}{2}
,
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<1
, and
T>0
,
let
w
be
(\rho,\gamma)
-irregular on
[0,T]
in the sense of Definition
1.1
.
Suppose that
s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy (
1.25
)
and fix

u_{0}\in H^{s}(\mathbb{T})

and
\phi\in\HS(L^{2}_{0}(\mathbb{T});H^{s}_{0}(\mathbb{T}))
.
Then, as
N\to\infty
,
the solution
\mathbf{u}^{N}

to the following truncated dynamics
:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}^{N}=\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)^{-1}\partial_{x}\big((\mathbf{P}_{N}U^{w}(t)\mathbf{u}^{N})^{2}\big)+U^{w}(t)^{-1}\phi\xi\\
\mathbf{u}^{N}|_{t=0}=u_{0}\end{cases}

(9.15)

converges to
the modulated interaction
\mathbf{u}(t)=U^{w}(t)^{-1}u(t)

of the solution
u

to
the stochastic modulated KdV equation (
1.30
)
with
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}

in
\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}([0,\tau];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
,
where
\tau>0
denotes the almost surely positive local existence time
from Theorem
1.21
(i)

(
but
possibly made smaller by a multiplicative constant
)
.

It is worthwhile to note that while we have
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{N}\in\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}([0,\tau];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))

only for
\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
,
the difference

\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{u}^{N}

belongs to a better space

\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}([0,\tau];H^{s}(\mathbb{T}))
(with
\gamma>\frac{1}{2}
).

Proof of Lemma
9.4
.

Let
X
and
X^{N}
be as in
(
4.3
) and
(
4.7
), respectively.
Then,
from Proposition
4.1
(iv),
we see that

X^{N}
converges to
X

in
\mathcal{X}^{s,\gamma}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T})
as
N\to\infty
.

Note that
\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{u}^{N}
satisfy the following equations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{u}(t)&=u_{0}+\mathcal{I}^{X}(\mathbf{u})(t)+\Psi,\\
\mathbf{u}^{N}(t)&=u_{0}+\mathcal{I}^{X^{N}}(\mathbf{u}^{N})(t)+\Psi,\end{split}

(9.16)

where
\Psi
is as in (
1.38
) and (
9.1
).
In particular,
\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{u}^{N}
satisfies (
3.67
).
Then, by repeating the proof of
Proposition
3.8
(iii),
we obtain the desired convergence.
∎
